General Ledger Suspense and Clearing Controls
1. Q: What is the purpose of a General Ledger Suspense and Clearing Accounts Standard Operating Procedure (SOP)?
A: Show answer
The purpose is to establish a controlled, evidence-based process for identifying, investigating, matching, correcting, reconciling, and resolving suspense and clearing balances without compromising ledger integrity. The SOP should define which accounts may be used, which transactions may enter them, who owns each unresolved item, what evidence is required, how clearance decisions are approved, and when an item can be closed. It should also establish escalation, reporting, record-retention, and monitoring requirements. A suspense balance must never be cleared merely because it is old, small, inconvenient, or temporarily reduced to zero. For the actual organization, the SOP must be based on the applicable accounting framework, approved finance policies, legal requirements, account configuration, and delegated authority. The supplied document is explicitly a fictional demonstration, not an adopted organizational policy.

Study instruction:
- "Review the complete lifecycle of a suspense item and identify the evidence, control owner, approval, reconciliation, and closure requirement at each stage."
Key point:
- Clearance requires evidence of the correct accounting outcome, not simply a reduction in the account balance.
- Fictional examples must not be treated as approved organizational instructions.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger
2. Q: What is the difference between a suspense account, a clearing account, an interface transit account, and a control account?
A: Show answer
A suspense account temporarily holds an accounting item whose nature, classification, ownership, or destination has not yet been established adequately. A clearing account records transactions awaiting a defined matching, settlement, transfer, or allocation process. An interface transit account captures movements between systems or processing stages when the receiving outcome still requires verification. A control account summarizes or reconciles transactions recorded in a related subsidiary ledger or controlled population. These purposes are not interchangeable. Each account must have an approved purpose, permitted transaction types, source system, expected resolution event, and reconciliation method. Under the UN General Ledger process, reconciliation accounts linked to subsidiary ledgers have source restrictions, and open-item-managed accounts require corresponding debit and credit items to be matched. The actual account design must follow the applicable ERP architecture and accounting policy; an account caption alone does not determine the appropriate treatment.

Study instruction:
- "Classify several sample transactions into suspense, clearing, interface transit, or control-account categories and explain the evidence that supports each classification."
Key point:
- The approved purpose and transaction substance determine the appropriate account use.
- Restricted reconciliation accounts must follow their designated posting route.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and reconciliation accounts
3. Q: Which accounting authority and organizational requirements should govern suspense and clearing procedures?
A: Show answer
The organization must first establish its legal identity, reporting framework, applicable jurisdiction, and approved internal accounting policies. Mandatory legislation and the applicable accounting framework govern their respective areas. Donor agreements, partner contracts, and grant restrictions must be evaluated for the obligations they create, without treating contractual provisions as a substitute for mandatory accounting requirements. Approved internal procedures and ERP configurations must implement, rather than override, applicable authority. An international NGO must not assume that IPSAS applies merely because its work resembles that of a public-sector organization. Similarly, it must not assume that IFRS or a particular Indonesian accounting standard applies without establishing the entity's requirements. The source document appropriately treats the framework and actual-entity requirements as unresolved until formally verified. If a material question remains uncertain, the affected decision should be held for competent review rather than resolved through an unsupported spreadsheet assumption.

Study instruction:
- "Construct an authority hierarchy using the actual entity's legal form, reporting framework, finance manual, contracts, and applicable laws, and identify unresolved applicability questions."
Key point:
- Accounting treatment must follow the framework applicable to the actual reporting entity.
- Unverified legal, donor, and policy requirements must remain explicit adoption gates.
Official links:
- IPSASB, 2026 Handbook of International Public Sector Accounting Standards Board Pronouncements
4. Q: Why should suspense and clearing accounts have approved purposes and restricted permitted uses?
A: Show answer
Without a defined purpose, an account can become a general repository for unidentified receipts, unsupported journals, old balances, or transactions that should have been processed through an originating subledger. This increases classification errors, obscures outstanding obligations, and makes reconciliations less reliable. The account register should identify its purpose, account type, permitted source, prohibited use, required dimensions, responsible owner, normal balance characteristics where applicable, matching method, and approval route. Reconciliation accounts and other restricted accounts may require posting only through designated modules or approved interfaces. Where direct journal entries are prohibited, the restriction should be enforced by system configuration or other demonstrably effective controls. A documented rule that users can bypass is insufficient. Account additions or changes should be approved, tested, and reflected in the controlled SOP before implementation.

Study instruction:
- "Inspect the actual chart of accounts and account configuration, then test each suspense and clearing account against its approved purpose and prohibited posting routes."
Key point:
- Account purpose must determine permitted processing and posting routes.
- Restricted account controls must be tested in the actual operating system.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Reconciliation accounts and General Ledger entry sources
5. Q: How should responsibilities be divided across suspense-item investigation, journal preparation, approval, posting, reconciliation, and closure?
A: Show answer
Responsibility should be divided among the transaction originator, item owner, investigator, general-ledger accountant, journal reviewer, authorized approver, posting operator, reconciliation preparer, reconciliation reviewer, and any relevant source-process owner or specialist. The person investigating an item should not automatically have authority to approve a material correction, execute the posting, and certify the reconciliation. Delegation must be verified for the actual individual, legal entity, transaction type, amount, currency, period, and effective date. A job title alone does not prove authority. Where limited staffing prevents full separation, an independently performed compensating review should be defined with its scope, reviewer, timing, and evidence. Internal Audit should evaluate the control independently rather than become the operator of the routine process. Responsibility must remain clear even when work is referred to another department or external adviser.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a RACI matrix for investigation, disposition, journal approval, posting verification, reconciliation, and closure, then identify any self-approval conflicts."
Key point:
- Preparation, approval, execution, and independent verification should be appropriately separated.
- Any compensating control must be documented and demonstrated to operate.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger process and journal workflows
6. Q: What information should be recorded when a new suspense or clearing item is identified?
A: Show answer
Each item should receive a durable, unique identifier and a recorded discovery date. The register should identify the legal entity, ledger, account code, currency, amount, debit or credit direction, transaction date, accounting period, original journal or interface reference, source system, counterparty where known, source-document references, project or fund dimensions, current owner, status, next action, due date, and escalation level. It should distinguish verified facts from provisional explanations and record which supporting evidence is missing. The originating transaction must remain traceable when an item is split, combined, reclassified, referred, reopened, or partially settled. Corrections should create a history rather than overwrite the initial record. Required fields and status values should be controlled so that incomplete entries cannot silently progress to clearance or reconciliation sign-off.

Study instruction:
- "Design an item register and test whether an independent reviewer can trace each record to its original transaction, responsible owner, current status, and next action."
Key point:
- Every unresolved item needs a unique identity and traceable origin.
- Missing critical information must prevent unsupported clearance.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger documents and processing
7. Q: How should a suspense item be classified and routed to the correct source process?
A: Show answer
Classification should identify the underlying event rather than infer its nature from the account caption alone. Possible origins include unidentified bank receipts, duplicate imports, accounts-payable items, accounts-receivable settlements, interoffice transfers, payroll interfaces, inventory movements, foreign-currency transactions, and other posting or mapping problems. Once the origin is sufficiently established, the item should be assigned to the responsible source process or specialist. An accounts-payable error, for example, generally requires investigation and correction in the appropriate AP workflow, not merely a balancing entry in a suspense account. The referral must identify the issue, source evidence, receiving owner, required action, due date, and acceptance status. The suspense owner retains monitoring responsibility until the referral is accepted and the actual accounting resolution is evidenced. Unknown ownership or conflicting classifications require a controlled hold.

Study instruction:
- "Use several fictional transaction records to practice classification, routing, referral acceptance, and verification of the originating system's correction."
Key point:
- Items should be resolved through the correct source process wherever applicable.
- Referral alone does not constitute accounting clearance.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Interactions between General Ledger and subsidiary ledgers
8. Q: How should an organization distinguish a genuine suspense item from a transaction that belongs in an existing subledger?
A: Show answer
The investigator should establish whether the transaction belongs to an established accounts-payable, accounts-receivable, payroll, fixed-asset, inventory, grants, tax, or other controlled process. Review the underlying contract, invoice, remittance advice, bank record, payroll output, source-system reference, and relevant account rules. If the originating process already owns the transaction, the correction should normally follow that process's approved workflow. A general-ledger adjustment should not be used to bypass required supplier, customer, employee, or asset records. In systems where reconciliation accounts receive postings only from subsidiary ledgers, direct journal entries must be prevented. If the source is unknown, the investigation must gather evidence before deciding whether the item belongs in suspense or another account. The final classification should explain both the transaction's economic substance and the reason for the chosen route.

Study instruction:
- "Compare a direct GL journal with a transaction originating in AP or AR and identify the authoritative source record and posting restrictions in the applicable system."
Key point:
- An item should not bypass a required subledger merely because a GL entry is faster.
- The permitted posting route depends on the actual ledger design and approved accounting process.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Subsidiary ledgers and reconciliation accounts
9. Q: What evidence is sufficient to investigate and resolve an outstanding suspense item?
A: Show answer
Evidence should be appropriate to the transaction and sufficient to support the identity of the event, its value, counterparties, accounting classification, authorization, and final outcome. Depending on the case, this may include bank statements, payment instructions, remittance advice, contracts, invoices, delivery confirmations, payroll records, interoffice confirmations, interface source rows, original journals, receiving-system records, and approved correction documents. An email or verbal explanation may help direct the investigation, but it should not replace mandatory source evidence or formal authorization. The register should identify the evidence actually inspected, not merely state that a document exists. Conflicting, incomplete, altered, or unverifiable records must be escalated. Evidence should also demonstrate the final outcome, including persisted postings, settlement, allocation, and any residual balance. The required evidence should follow the approved policy and transaction type.

Study instruction:
- "Select three unresolved items and prepare an evidence matrix showing what is available, what is missing, what each document proves, and what is required before clearance."
Key point:
- Evidence must support both the proposed accounting treatment and the completed outcome.
- Narrative explanations alone may be insufficient for authorization or clearance.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger documents and accounting evidence
10. Q: Which matching criteria should be applied before two accounting records are treated as the same transaction?
A: Show answer
Matching should use the strongest available identifiers, including legal entity, currency, amount, originating document, transaction reference, counterparty, account, source system, relevant dates, and required fund or project dimensions. The process should distinguish mandatory keys from supporting attributes. A conflicting legal entity, currency, counterparty, or source reference may indicate a different transaction even when the amount is identical. Date proximity and amount equality can generate candidates but do not independently establish identity. The matching rules should define required attributes, acceptable variations, permitted one-to-one or many-to-one relationships, and how exceptions are reviewed. Each confirmed match should retain links to the original records and a record of the decision. If a required key conflicts or the evidence cannot establish identity, the item should remain open or on hold rather than being automatically cleared.

Study instruction:
- "Build test cases with identical amounts but different counterparties, currencies, entities, and references, and confirm that the matching process blocks invalid combinations."
Key point:
- Matching must establish transaction identity, not merely numerical similarity.
- Hard-key conflicts should prevent automatic clearance.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Open-item clearing and General Ledger processing
11. Q: Why is amount-only matching unsafe for suspense and clearing accounts?
A: Show answer
Many unrelated transactions can have the same amount. Matching only by value may incorrectly connect an unidentified bank receipt to an invoice, an interoffice movement to a supplier payment, or a duplicate record to a legitimate settlement. The risk increases when amounts are rounded, repeated, or generated by recurring payments. A stronger matching rule combines monetary values with the correct currency, entity, counterparty, transaction identity, timing, and source evidence. The system should flag amount-only similarities as candidates for investigation rather than approve them automatically. A reviewer must determine whether any apparent match is supported by an originating document and an appropriate accounting relationship. Matching tolerances, if permitted, must be approved and configured explicitly; an unapproved tolerance must not authorize clearance.

Study instruction:
- "Create a sample dataset containing unrelated transactions with equal amounts and compare the results of amount-only matching with evidence-based matching."
Key point:
- Equal values do not prove that transactions are related.
- Matching tolerances require approved parameters and review controls.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Open Item Managed account clearing
12. Q: How should fuzzy matching and similarity scores be used when references or descriptions are inconsistent?
A: Show answer
Fuzzy matching can help identify candidate relationships when bank narratives, invoice references, counterparty names, or descriptions differ slightly. It can normalize case, remove harmless punctuation differences, and flag similar text or nearby amounts for investigation. It must not independently authorize a journal, settlement, or closure. The process should display the original fields, transformed fields, matching rationale, confidence or similarity measure, and unresolved discrepancies. The reviewer should inspect authoritative supporting evidence and confirm that the records relate to the same economic event. A high similarity score cannot override a conflicting counterparty, currency, legal entity, or transaction reference. The procedure should also record false positives and false negatives so matching rules can be improved without weakening mandatory controls.

Study instruction:
- "Test fuzzy matching with misspelled names, altered references, and unrelated transactions that have similar descriptions, and explain why human verification remains necessary."
Key point:
- Similarity is a search aid, not proof of transaction identity.
- Hard-key conflicts and insufficient evidence must continue to block clearance.
13. Q: How should duplicate transactions and repeated interface imports be detected and handled?
A: Show answer
Duplicate detection should examine transaction identity, source-system identifiers, references, values, dates, counterparties, component identifiers, and interface batch information. The review should distinguish a genuine duplicate from two legitimate transactions sharing the same reference or amount. A suspected duplicate should be flagged with both original and candidate records, the identifying attributes, and the proposed disposition. The process should not delete or overwrite source records merely because they appear duplicated. If duplication is confirmed, determine whether a source-system correction, reversal, journal adjustment, or interface remediation is required. The resulting action must preserve the original transaction, correction link, approval, and posting evidence. Duplicate prevention should also address idempotency and retry behavior so that an interface does not create a second posting when the original result is uncertain.

Study instruction:
- "Run duplicate tests using the same reference, different references for the same transaction, and legitimate repeated payments, then verify that the workflow distinguishes all three cases."
Key point:
- Suspected duplicates require evidence-based review before removal or correction.
- Retry prevention and correction linkage are separate controls that work together.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger document processing
14. Q: How should an organization investigate a partial, split, or combined transaction match?
A: Show answer
A partial settlement occurs when only part of the original amount has been resolved. A split match allocates one source item to several destination components, while a combined match connects multiple valid source components to one settlement or destination record. Each component requires a unique identifier, evidence of the relationship, and an independently verifiable amount. The component values must reconcile to the original source amount or to the approved settlement basis. When a payment of IDR 2,400 is only partially supported by confirmed settlement of IDR 1,500, the remaining IDR 900 must stay open unless separate evidence establishes another valid disposition. A combined settlement must not reuse a source component already assigned to another match. Matching records should retain the full allocation map and the residual calculation.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate partial, split, and combined matches independently and verify that each source component is used only once and every residual remains correctly identified."
Key point:
- Partial settlement does not justify closing the entire original balance.
- Allocation components must reconcile without duplicate use or unexplained residuals.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Open Item Managed account clearing
15. Q: How should transactions originating in interfaces between operational systems and the General Ledger be controlled?
A: Show answer
An interface transfers data from a source application to a receiving application. The control process should establish the expected source population, batch identifier, extraction time, record count, total control amount, rejected records, retry history, and receiving-system results. The receiving records must be compared with the originating records to establish completeness and accuracy. Partial imports, failed records, stale extracts, invalid dimensions, duplicate messages, and processing delays must be investigated. A successful file transfer does not prove that every accounting entry was persisted correctly. Any unresolved posting status must remain visible until the target system is checked. The interface owner, source-process owner, and GL control owner should have explicit responsibilities for investigation and correction. Month-end processing must include verification that relevant interface data is current, correct, and complete before reconciliation and sign-off.

Study instruction:
- "Reconcile a sample interface batch from source extraction through import, rejection handling, persisted postings, and GL totals."
Key point:
- Interface completion must be supported by receiving-system evidence and reconciled control totals.
- Unknown or partial outcomes must not trigger an unsafe retry.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger interfaces and closing process
16. Q: What should happen when an interface times out and the posting outcome is unknown?
A: Show answer
The operator must not assume that a timeout means the transaction failed. The transaction may have been persisted even though the response never reached the sending application. Preserve the original request, batch ID, unique transaction key, timestamp, system response, and error logs. Query the receiving system for the actual persisted outcome and search for possible duplicates before deciding whether to retry. If the target system confirms that no posting occurred and the approved retry conditions are met, a controlled retry may proceed. If the result remains unknown or the system does not provide reliable status evidence, hold the transaction and escalate to the system owner. Record the final status and any corrective action. Idempotency controls should prevent repeated execution of the same logical transaction where the system supports them, but they must be verified in the actual configuration.

Study instruction:
- "Simulate a timeout after a transaction may have been committed and document how the receiving system, duplicate check, retry decision, and final reconciliation establish the outcome."
Key point:
- A timeout is not proof that a posting failed.
- Persisted status and duplicate-safety checks must precede any retry.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger processing and interface controls
17. Q: How should the age of an unresolved suspense item be calculated and controlled?
A: Show answer
The organization should establish an approved aging convention, including the relevant discovery or transaction date, calendar-day or business-day basis, and the treatment of weekends and holidays. The register should retain the original discovery date and current age even when an item is reassigned, reclassified, referred, extended, or reopened. Otherwise, users can make old items appear new simply by changing ownership or status. Aging profiles should define investigation milestones, escalation thresholds, accountable owners, and required follow-up. Aging is a prioritization mechanism, not an accounting treatment or authorization to write off a balance. High-risk items may require escalation immediately, regardless of age. The actual thresholds and service levels must be approved for the organization rather than copied from fictional examples or assumed to be universally appropriate.

Study instruction:
- "Calculate item ages from original dates and test how reassignment, extensions, reclassification, and reopening affect aging reports."
Key point:
- Original age must remain visible throughout the item's lifecycle.
- Escalation thresholds need approved definitions and accountable owners.
18. Q: Under what circumstances may an overdue suspense item receive an extension?
A: Show answer
An extension should be permitted only when the remaining work, reason for delay, current risk, outstanding evidence, responsible owner, revised action plan, and new target date are documented. The extension must receive approval from an authorized person and should not remove or conceal the original due date. Multiple extensions should remain visible so managers can distinguish legitimate external delays from weak follow-up or recurring process failures. Extensions must not override a statutory deadline, contractual obligation, period-close requirement, or other mandatory deadline. Items with potential fraud, material misstatement, restricted funding, or unauthorized posting should follow the relevant escalation route regardless of any administrative extension. Reviewers should examine repeated extensions and require root-cause action when delays recur. The system should retain the full history of original and revised target dates.

Study instruction:
- "Evaluate examples of overdue items and decide whether extensions are justified, properly authorized, and consistent with other applicable deadlines."
Key point:
- An extension changes a target date only within approved authority; it does not erase aging history.
- Repeated extensions require scrutiny and may indicate a control weakness.
19. Q: What status workflow should be used to manage suspense items from identification through final closure?
A: Show answer
A controlled lifecycle may include received, classified, assigned, investigating, awaiting evidence, on hold, referred, match proposed, awaiting review, approved, posting pending, posting verified, partially cleared, reconciled, closed, reopened, or escalated. These labels are illustrative and must be adapted to the approved workflow. Every status should have an entry criterion, permitted next states, authorized role, evidence requirement, and exit criterion. An item must not move directly from received to closed without the required investigation, approval, accounting outcome, reconciliation, and closure checks. A partially cleared item must retain its residual balance and remain open. Reopening should preserve the original closure record and explain the new evidence or reason. Status definitions should be implemented consistently in the register and system, with unauthorized transitions blocked or detected.

Study instruction:
- "Build a status-transition matrix and test whether invalid shortcuts, premature closure, and unsupported reopening are blocked or recorded."
Key point:
- Every status transition requires defined authorization and evidence.
- A workflow status is not, by itself, proof that the underlying accounting action occurred.
20. Q: What conditions must be satisfied before a suspense or clearing item can be considered cleared?
A: Show answer
Clearance requires an established transaction identity, sufficient source evidence, an approved accounting disposition, valid authority, the correct account and dimensions, a balanced and authorized journal where applicable, and confirmation of the actual outcome in the receiving system. The item must then be matched or settled, any residual amount explained and tracked, and the relevant account or item-register reconciliation completed. An independent reviewer should confirm that the evidence supports the decision and that closure follows the approved policy. The closure record should link to the original item, source documents, approval, posting reference, resulting balance, and reconciliation. A zero balance or a “closed” status alone is insufficient. If any mandatory element remains uncertain, the item should remain on hold or open until the issue is resolved through an authorized process.

Study instruction:
- "Apply the clearance checklist to a completed item and a deliberately incomplete case, then identify which missing evidence prevents closure."
Key point:
- Clearance requires supported accounting resolution and independently reviewable evidence.
- Zero balance, elapsed time, or referral does not replace the closure criteria.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Open-item clearing and reconciliation
21. Q: How should correcting journals and reclassifications be prepared for suspense items?
A: Show answer
The preparer should establish the original transaction, identify the incorrect or unresolved accounting treatment, determine the supported destination account, and explain why the correction is needed. The journal should include the correct legal entity, account, currency, period, project or fund dimensions where applicable, source reference, original journal link, calculation, and supporting documentation. Debit and credit totals must balance, but a balanced journal is not necessarily correct: the classification, authorization, period, and economic substance must also be valid. An independent reviewer should check the rationale and evidence before approval and posting. Source records should not be altered merely to make the GL agree. If the correction affects a donor report, tax obligation, subledger, or previously closed period, the corresponding specialist and approved correction procedure must also be considered.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a sample correcting journal from source evidence and test its account mapping, dimensions, period, debit-credit balance, approval, and effect on the original item."
Key point:
- A balanced journal still requires correct classification, evidence, and authorization.
- Corrections should preserve links to the original accounting records.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger document processing and adjustments
22. Q: When should a reversal be used instead of a correcting reclassification?
A: Show answer
A reversal is generally used to negate an earlier entry in accordance with the applicable accounting policy or approved transaction procedure. A correcting reclassification changes the accounting destination or dimensions while preserving the underlying economic event. The appropriate method depends on the original error, required accounting treatment, system capabilities, period status, and policy. The correction should identify the original entry, reason, approving authority, reversal or replacement reference, and the resulting balance. A reversal must not be used to hide an unsupported transaction, bypass an approval, or erase the audit history. Likewise, a reclassification should not disguise an error that requires a full reversal or another formal correction route. If the period is closed, the responsible controller must determine the permitted correction procedure before any posting occurs.

Study instruction:
- "Compare an incorrect account classification with an invalid original transaction and determine which correction approach is supported by the applicable policy."
Key point:
- Reversals and reclassifications have different purposes and must be properly justified.
- Original and corrective records must remain linked and traceable.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger adjustments and period-end processing
23. Q: How should a suspense balance be handled when another department owns the originating transaction?
A: Show answer
The suspense owner should document the identified source process, available evidence, outstanding question, requested action, receiving owner, agreed deadline, and referral reference. The receiving department should acknowledge the handover and take responsibility for its specific action. However, operational referral does not establish that the GL item has been resolved. The suspense owner should continue monitoring the balance until the source correction or settlement has actually occurred, the relevant posting is verified, and the account reconciliation supports closure. If the receiving department rejects the referral or misses the agreed deadline, the item should be escalated under the approved procedure. Long-running referrals should be visible in aging reports and management reviews, with root-cause analysis for recurring ownership disputes.

Study instruction:
- "Trace an item referred to AP, AR, Treasury, or another source owner and confirm that the final source correction and GL outcome are evidenced before closure."
Key point:
- A referral transfers a task or responsibility but does not automatically clear the accounting balance.
- Closure requires evidence of the final source and ledger outcome.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and subsidiary-ledger interactions
24. Q: How should an opening suspense balance with incomplete historical supporting documents be investigated?
A: Show answer
Preserve the original opening balance, account, period, available journal references, discovery date, and records of earlier investigations. Search approved archives, historical reconciliations, bank records, source-system transactions, correspondence, and related-party confirmations. Establish which facts can be independently corroborated and which remain unknown. Do not fabricate a transaction explanation or assume that the balance is a valid asset, liability, income, or expense solely because it appears in the ledger. If the original source cannot be established, escalate the case for accounting, legal, or other specialist review as appropriate. Any proposed correction or write-off must follow the applicable framework, policy, materiality considerations, and delegated authority. The unresolved balance and the decision process should remain visible in reporting until an authorized, evidenced disposition is completed.

Study instruction:
- "Investigate a fictional legacy balance using an evidence log and distinguish verified facts, hypotheses, missing documentation, and decisions that require authorization."
Key point:
- An unexplained opening balance does not establish the correct accounting treatment.
- Historical uncertainty must be documented and resolved through an authorized process.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and reconciliation
25. Q: How should a suspense balance be reconciled from the opening balance through the closing balance?
A: Show answer
Reconciliation should independently calculate the closing account balance from the opening balance and all in-period debit and credit movements according to the ledger's sign convention. The calculation should be compared with the actual GL closing balance and the total of item-level details. For example, if the illustrative opening balance is IDR 5,000, debits are IDR 2,000, and credits are IDR 3,300, the closing balance is IDR 3,700 under a debit-positive convention. That amount must then agree with the independently summed open-item register, with any differences investigated rather than plugged. The reconciliation should identify all source transactions, journals, reversals, reclassifications, and outstanding items. A reviewer must verify completeness, arithmetic, source integrity, and the disposition of residual differences before sign-off.

Study instruction:
- "Calculate a full roll-forward independently and reconcile it to the GL, item register, and source records; document the resolution of any difference."
Key point:
- Opening balance plus valid movements must reconcile to the reported closing balance.
- The GL and item register must agree independently, not through copied totals.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger reconciliation and clearing
26. Q: How can an organization prove that the item register is complete and agrees with the General Ledger?
A: Show answer
Establish a controlled population of relevant GL accounts and obtain the full period-end transaction population from the authoritative ledger. Compare account balances and movement totals with the item register using account, legal entity, currency, period, journal reference, and item identifier. Investigate GL entries without register items, register items without corresponding GL records, duplicated details, unallocated components, and items assigned to an incorrect account. Calculate the register total independently rather than importing the GL closing balance into the register's total field. Where mapping is required, show every source item, destination account, mapping rule, amount, and unresolved component. The final reconciliation should demonstrate complete coverage, correct arithmetic, valid mappings, and independent review. No unresolved discrepancy should be hidden by an unsupported adjustment or offset.

Study instruction:
- "Perform a bidirectional reconciliation from GL to register and from register to GL, then quantify missing, extra, duplicated, and incorrectly mapped records."
Key point:
- Reconciliation must test completeness and accuracy in both directions.
- Source population and register totals should be independently established.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and clearing processes
27. Q: Why is a zero balance insufficient evidence that a suspense item has been resolved?
A: Show answer
A zero balance can result from valid settlement, but it can also result from an unsupported offset, an accidental reversal, an incorrect account mapping, duplicated credits, or an unauthorized adjustment. The reviewer must inspect the underlying transactions and determine whether the balance reached zero for the correct reason. Evidence should establish the original event, approved disposition, actual posting, related settlement, and any residual treatment. Where open-item management is used, the corresponding debits and credits should be matched according to the permitted system process. An item should not be marked cleared merely because net debit and credit values cancel numerically. If the account has a zero balance but unresolved item records remain, investigate whether those items are legitimate outstanding details, duplicates, or improperly matched entries.

Study instruction:
- "Review a zero-balance account containing an unsupported adjustment and explain which transaction and closure evidence are still required."
Key point:
- Zero balance is a numerical condition, not proof of correct clearance.
- Transaction matching and supporting evidence remain necessary.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Open Item Managed accounts and clearing
28. Q: How should suspense and clearing items be controlled at month-end and year-end?
A: Show answer
The close process should establish the authoritative transaction population, confirm relevant interface completeness, reconcile account movements, identify all remaining open items, review material adjustments, and document unresolved exceptions. Each outstanding item should have a named owner, current balance, original age, explanation, next action, target date, and escalation status. The reconciliation preparer and reviewer should be independent where required by the control design. Items must not be cleared solely to make the year-end balance sheet appear tidy. Material unidentified balances, restricted-fund issues, old unsupported entries, and posting uncertainties should be escalated to the controller before certification. If the reporting period is closed, any late adjustment must follow an approved reopening or subsequent-period correction procedure. The process should retain final reconciliations, supporting schedules, approvals, and the actual closing ledger extract.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a month-end and year-end checklist that links interface completeness, account roll-forward, open-item aging, journal approval, and reconciliation sign-off."
Key point:
- Period-end clearance must preserve accounting integrity and the complete audit trail.
- Unresolved items require visible treatment and authorized escalation, not artificial elimination.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger month-end and year-end closing
29. Q: How should a correction be handled when the accounting period is already closed?
A: Show answer
First establish the error, original period, materiality, affected balances, reporting consequences, and applicable accounting policy. Then determine whether the correction requires an authorized period reopening, a posting in the current period, or another policy-compliant procedure. The decision must consider legal reporting requirements, donor reporting, financial statements, and any effect on previously approved reports. Users should not backdate a journal or reopen a period outside delegated authority. The correction package should document the original entry, rationale, proposed treatment, approval, resulting posting, and effect on reconciliations. If the issue changes a previously issued report or filed return, the relevant correction process must also be evaluated. The source document intentionally treats period-reopen authority as a configurable control parameter that must be approved for the actual entity.

Study instruction:
- "Review a fictional error discovered after closing and document the permitted correction route, required approvals, affected reports, and evidence of completion."
Key point:
- Closed-period corrections require a policy-supported and authorized route.
- Original reporting records must remain traceable after correction.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger adjustments and period-end processing
30. Q: How should foreign-currency suspense items be measured and cleared?
A: Show answer
The organization must preserve the original currency, foreign-currency amount, functional-currency amount, transaction date, applicable exchange-rate source, rate convention, and relevant posting references. Matching should not equate a foreign-currency amount to a different currency solely because the converted values appear close. The investigator must identify the exchange-rate policy and applicable accounting requirements for the transaction and reporting date. Monetary balances may require remeasurement under the applicable framework, while the recognition and classification of resulting foreign-exchange differences depend on the relevant rules and facts. If the required rate or rate date has not been established, the adjustment should remain on hold rather than use an invented or unapproved rate. The calculation must be independently reproducible and the resulting journal reviewed before posting.

Study instruction:
- "Trace a fictional foreign-currency item from original amount through approved conversion, remeasurement if applicable, and final clearing, and independently reproduce the calculation."
Key point:
- Foreign-currency matching and valuation require the applicable currency and exchange-rate rules.
- An unverified exchange-rate parameter must not be used to authorize an adjustment.
Official links:
- IFRS Foundation — IAS 21, The Effects of Changes in Foreign Exchange Rates
31. Q: How should donor, grant, fund, project, and cost-center dimensions be handled when clearing suspense items?
A: Show answer
Each transaction should retain the dimensions required by the organization's approved accounting structure, reporting framework, grant agreements, and internal policies. These may include legal entity, donor, grant, project, activity, fund, cost center, or location. An item cannot be cleared into an arbitrary destination simply because the debits and credits balance. The reviewer should verify that the destination dimensions are valid, mutually compatible, and supported by the underlying transaction. Donor restrictions and eligible-cost rules must be checked where a correction affects grant reporting or fund use. If the correct dimensions cannot be established, the item should remain open or held for Grants Finance or another authorized specialist. Any reallocation must preserve the original record and include its calculation, rationale, approval, and impact on affected reports.

Study instruction:
- "Test a set of suspense items against a master list of approved project and donor codes and verify the treatment of missing or incompatible dimensions."
Key point:
- Correct account classification also requires the correct reporting dimensions.
- Donor eligibility and restrictions must be checked when relevant to the proposed disposition.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and reporting dimensions
32. Q: When should a suspense item be escalated to tax, legal, grants, treasury, or another specialist?
A: Show answer
Specialist review is appropriate when the issue involves legal or tax obligations, uncertain donor restrictions, foreign-currency treatment, contractual settlement requirements, unusual counterparties, suspected misconduct, or an accounting-framework question beyond the assigned owner's authority. The referral should summarize verified facts, unresolved questions, potential financial and compliance effects, source documents, deadlines, and the exact decision needed. The specialist's response should identify the applicable authority or agreement and the basis for the conclusion. The accounting owner must then assess the operational and financial consequences, obtain any additional approval, and link the decision to the affected item. A specialist referral does not itself authorize a journal or close the balance. High-risk cases must remain held until the required conclusion and approval are documented.

Study instruction:
- "Classify several exception cases by specialist requirement and draft a referral that specifies the question, evidence, risk, deadline, and expected decision."
Key point:
- Specialist review is required when the decision exceeds the item owner's verified authority or expertise.
- The resulting accounting action still needs the appropriate approval and evidence.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and accounting-control processes
33. Q: Under what conditions can a residual suspense balance be written off?
A: Show answer
A residual should be considered for write-off only after reasonable investigation establishes its nature, the applicable accounting treatment, the reason it cannot be recovered or otherwise settled, and the relevant policy and authority. The review should consider the amount, age, supporting evidence, counterparty, legal or contractual implications, donor conditions, potential tax effects, and whether the balance indicates an underlying control weakness. Materiality may influence the level of review, but a small balance does not automatically justify elimination. The proposal should include an independently checked calculation, documented rationale, account and dimension treatment, required approvals, and an evaluation of the effect on reporting. The approved journal must remain linked to the original item. If evidence or authority is missing, the balance must remain open or on hold rather than be written off for convenience.

Study instruction:
- "Evaluate a fictional residual write-off proposal and identify the supporting evidence, approval level, specialist input, and reconciliation update required."
Key point:
- A write-off is an accounting decision that requires an appropriate basis and authority.
- Small value, old age, or operational inconvenience is not sufficient evidence by itself.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger adjustments
34. Q: How should suspected fraud, altered source documents, or unauthorized suspense postings be handled?
A: Show answer
The response should protect evidence, restrict inappropriate access where authorized, and invoke the organization's applicable incident-reporting, investigation, and safeguarding procedures. Record the suspected event, source record, transaction identifiers, dates, individuals or roles involved where appropriate, and the basis for concern. Preserve original files and relevant audit logs; do not overwrite evidence, confront suspected individuals outside the approved process, or alter accounting records merely to conceal the issue. The appropriate compliance, legal, fraud-response, or management authority should determine the investigation route and any necessary financial hold. The affected balance should not be cleared until the authorized process establishes a permitted accounting outcome. A final resolution should include evidence handling, approved corrections, any required notifications, and a separate assessment of the control failures that allowed the event to occur.

Study instruction:
- "Use a fictional altered-document scenario to identify the immediate evidence-preservation steps, reporting route, authority restrictions, and accounting controls that must remain in effect."
Key point:
- Suspected fraud or document alteration requires protected escalation and preservation of evidence.
- Routine clearance controls must not bypass an incident-response process.
35. Q: What audit-trail information should be retained for a suspense-item correction and closure?
A: Show answer
The audit trail should identify the original item, source records, evidence inspected, classification decision, investigation performed, matching rationale, proposed accounting treatment, preparer, reviewer, approver, posting operator, persisted posting result, reconciliation, closure decision, and timestamps. It should also retain previous and revised values, reasons for changes, reference links, and any status or ownership transitions. Corrections must be appended to the history rather than erase the original state. Access to alter or delete the trail should be restricted and monitored. Audit records must be retrievable for the applicable retention period and protected against unauthorized modification. If an item is reopened, the earlier closure remains part of the historical record. The required level of detail depends on the organization's systems, policy, legal obligations, and relevant reporting requirements.

Study instruction:
- "Reconstruct a completed suspense item using only its stored audit trail and determine whether an independent reviewer can reproduce the decision and final accounting outcome."
Key point:
- Every material decision and change should be traceable to an authorized person and supporting evidence.
- Reopening an item must preserve its earlier history and closure evidence.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger document processing
36. Q: How should access to suspense-account functions and sensitive accounting records be controlled?
A: Show answer
Access should be assigned according to job responsibilities and the minimum privileges necessary to perform authorized tasks. The access register should identify the user, role, permissions, approving authority, effective date, expiration or review date, and relevant system scope. Privileged users should not be able to independently authorize conflicting activities without an appropriate independent control. Access should be reviewed periodically and removed promptly when duties change or a user leaves. Shared credentials undermine accountability and should not be used to bypass required approvals. Sensitive counterparty, payroll, bank, or investigation records should be visible only to authorized personnel. Review access logs for unusual or unauthorized changes and preserve relevant evidence where exceptions are identified. System permissions must be tested in practice rather than inferred from role descriptions or documented policy alone.

Study instruction:
- "Compare the actual ERP permissions with the approved role matrix and test whether conflicting preparation, approval, posting, and closure activities can be performed by the same user."
Key point:
- Actual system access must align with approved responsibilities and segregation of duties.
- Access reviews and revocations should be evidenced and independently checked.
37. Q: How should management monitor suspense and clearing balances through regular reporting?
A: Show answer
Management reporting should present balances and open-item counts by account, legal entity, currency, age band, risk, source process, owner, and status. It should show newly identified items, items cleared, residual movements, overdue actions, unassigned records, items on hold, unresolved reconciliation differences, and cases requiring management decisions. Reports should distinguish the value of open balances from their age and risk, because a recently identified high-risk item may need more urgent attention than an old low-risk item. Management should also review recurring causes, repeated extensions, unauthorized routes, duplicate attempts, and unresolved source-system failures. Every reported metric should have a defined calculation, source population, owner, and reporting frequency. A decline in the closing balance must not be interpreted automatically as improved control quality.

Study instruction:
- "Design a suspense-account dashboard and reconcile its headline measures to the underlying GL and item register before presenting it to management."
Key point:
- Monitoring must show both financial exposure and the quality of outstanding control actions.
- A lower balance alone does not establish better reconciliation performance.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and financial reconciliation
38. Q: Which key performance indicators can measure whether suspense and clearing controls are working effectively?
A: Show answer
Useful indicators include the number and value of open items, aging distribution, proportion of items with valid owners and next actions, overdue-item rate, average investigation time, number of unsupported postings, unresolved source-to-register differences, duplicate-posting incidents, failed interface rows, repeat root causes, and the proportion of reconciliations reviewed on time. Each measure must have an explicit formula, defined population, reporting date, data owner, frequency, and target approved by management. For example, an overdue-item percentage should use the full population of applicable open items, not only those that have already been escalated. KPI results should be verified against the source register and GL because stale workflow status can create misleading dashboards. Trend analysis should trigger investigation and corrective action rather than reward superficial balance reduction or premature closure.

Study instruction:
- "Define each KPI mathematically, calculate it from a sample dataset, and verify the numerator and denominator against the underlying records."
Key point:
- KPIs need reproducible definitions and reliable source data.
- Performance measures should reward evidence-based resolution and control effectiveness, not artificial clearance.
39. Q: How should recurring suspense problems be investigated at the root-cause level?
A: Show answer
The investigation should distinguish the immediate cause of an individual balance from the underlying process defect that produced repeated exceptions. Causes may include poor source-data quality, incorrect account mapping, unclear ownership, inadequate interface controls, inconsistent transaction references, invalid system permissions, insufficient training, or weaknesses in approval workflows. The root-cause record should identify the affected process, frequency, financial or operational impact, contributing factors, corrective action, accountable owner, target date, and effectiveness test. Closing an individual item does not prove that its root cause has been eliminated. The organization should examine subsequent transactions to determine whether the control change prevents recurrence. Repeated symptoms require escalation when previous corrective actions have failed or were not fully implemented.

Study instruction:
- "Select a recurring suspense exception and trace it from individual transaction errors to the underlying process, system, or responsibility failure."
Key point:
- Item resolution and systemic remediation are separate control activities.
- Corrective actions require an owner, deadline, and effectiveness verification.
40. Q: How can Excel and Power Query support suspense-account reconciliation without changing the underlying accounting rules?
A: Show answer
Power Query can import controlled extracts from the GL, source systems, bank data, and the suspense register; standardize fields; normalize references; validate data types; combine master data; and identify candidate duplicates or unmatched records. Excel tables and formulas can calculate roll-forwards, aging, allocation totals, and control differences. The model should preserve original references, source filenames, extraction dates, query versions, and record counts. Automated outputs must be compared with independent calculations and authoritative source totals. Matching logic may identify exceptions, but it must not independently determine uncertain accounting treatments, override account restrictions, or authorize write-offs. Critical parameters such as currency tolerances and match windows must be approved and tested before being used operationally. Source tables, processing logic, and presentation dashboards should be separated so that users cannot accidentally alter raw records or formulas.

Study instruction:
- "Build a sample Power Query reconciliation from controlled source files and test missing fields, duplicated records, mismatched dimensions, and independent control totals."
Key point:
- Automation supports repeatability and exception detection but does not establish accounting validity.
- Transformations and matching rules must preserve source traceability and be independently tested.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and reconciliation process
- Microsoft Learn — What Is Power Query?
41. Q: How can VBA automate suspense-item workflows and journals while preserving appropriate approvals?
A: Show answer
VBA can generate exception reports, prepare draft journal templates, update controlled workflow fields, create reminder lists, export approved reports, and record automation-run metadata. It should not bypass authorization, choose unsupported account classifications, approve its own journals, or mark an item cleared simply because a macro completed successfully. The workflow should validate required fields, verify the account and dimension mapping, check debit-credit balance, detect stale approvals, and stop safely when inputs are missing or inconsistent. Posting functions must distinguish between a request being sent and the posting being persisted in the receiving system. The automation should retain version information, user identity where reliably available, timestamps, error details, and references to the underlying records. Changes to code or accounting parameters require controlled approval, testing, and deployment.

Study instruction:
- "Design a VBA process that prepares a draft correction, flags missing evidence, blocks unauthorized status changes, and records an error without treating failed posting as success."
Key point:
- VBA should support controlled preparation and monitoring, not replace accounting judgment or authorization.
- Automation success must be distinguished from verified transaction persistence.
Official links:
- Microsoft Learn — Excel VBA Reference
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger document workflows
42. Q: What validation and testing should be completed before a suspense-account reconciliation workbook is used operationally?
A: Show answer
Testing should cover required fields, data types, unique identifiers, permitted account mappings, mandatory dimensions, matching rules, duplicate detection, foreign-currency handling, date boundaries, debit-credit balancing, aging, role separation, approval freshness, and closure gates. Positive tests should demonstrate that valid cases can progress through the workflow. Negative tests should confirm that invalid account routes, amount-only matches, duplicate components, missing evidence, unauthorized approvals, unbalanced journals, and unknown posting outcomes are blocked or flagged. Independent calculations should be used to verify totals and allocation logic. The test inventory should identify the expected result, actual result, severity, evidence, and unresolved defect for every test. Technical checks should also confirm that the workbook or generated document can be opened, its structure remains intact, and its outputs are reproducible. Passing structural tests does not establish the validity of the accounting policy or prove that production permissions are correctly configured.

Study instruction:
- "Build a test register with positive and negative cases, record actual results, and independently verify calculations before release."
Key point:
- Testing must verify both correct processing and effective rejection of unsafe cases.
- Structural validation does not replace framework approval, production testing, or independent review.
Official links:
- Microsoft Learn — Excel VBA Reference
43. Q: How should a suspense account be handled when the original bank receipt cannot be identified?
A: Show answer
The investigator should obtain the original bank statement line, value date, amount, currency, transaction reference, remitter information, and any associated bank advice. Compare the receipt against expected funding, customer or donor remittances, interoffice transfers, refunds, and other plausible source records without treating similarity as proof. Contact the appropriate internal or external party through approved channels if necessary, and record each inquiry, response, and outstanding dependency. The item should remain in suspense while its nature and correct classification are unresolved. It must not automatically become revenue, unrestricted funding, a receivable, or another account solely to clear the bank reconciliation. Once a valid source is established, document the supported accounting treatment, approval, posting, and reconciliation. Any residual or disputed portion should remain separately identifiable.

Study instruction:
- "Investigate an unidentified fictional bank receipt and produce a traceable decision record showing the evidence that supports the final classification."
Key point:
- A bank receipt is not sufficient evidence to determine revenue or funding classification.
- Unidentified amounts remain open until the source and accounting treatment are adequately supported.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and reconciliation processes
44. Q: How should an interoffice transfer be investigated when the sending office confirms payment but the receiving office has no corresponding record?
A: Show answer
Establish the sending transaction's authorization, date, currency, amount, reference, and accounting entry. Then obtain receiving-office statements, bank or cash evidence where relevant, and the corresponding ledger or interface records. The difference may result from timing, an incorrect reference, a transfer fee, a failed interface, a wrong receiving entity, or incomplete records; the actual cause must be evidenced. Confirm whether the sending and receiving sides have used compatible accounts and dimensions. A sending-side confirmation alone does not prove that the receiving side was correctly posted or that the interoffice balance is fully settled. Retain the item as unresolved until both sides and any difference are reconciled. If the transfer is split or only partly confirmed, allocate the confirmed component and continue tracking the outstanding residual separately.

Study instruction:
- "Reconcile a fictional interoffice transfer from both entities' records and explain how partial confirmation, timing differences, and fees affect the open balance."
Key point:
- Interoffice clearance requires consistent evidence for both sides of the transaction.
- Partial confirmation does not justify treating the entire transfer as settled.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Inter-agency reconciliation and General Ledger
45. Q: How should an account restriction be enforced when a journal upload or manual entry could bypass the intended posting route?
A: Show answer
The control design should identify the permitted posting channels for each account and disable unauthorized routes where the system allows. This may require configuration restrictions, role-based permissions, blocked journal mappings, validation checks, or controlled review of approved manual-adjustment accounts. Any permitted exception route must specify the eligible transaction types, required source evidence, approving authority, and reconciliation method. A user should not be able to post directly to a restricted reconciliation account merely because a journal upload accepts the account code. The organization must test the actual runtime behavior, including file uploads, manual journals, interfaces, privileged-user access, and emergency procedures. If the control is only described in the SOP but not enforced or independently monitored, management should recognize the gap and implement a compensating control with an accountable owner and review evidence.

Study instruction:
- "Test prohibited posting routes in a controlled environment and document whether each attempt is blocked, detected, approved as an exception, and reconciled."
Key point:
- Restricted-account rules require effective operational enforcement.
- A written prohibition does not prove that the system prevents unauthorized posting.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Reconciliation-account posting restrictions
46. Q: What forms and registers should support a controlled suspense and clearing SOP?
A: Show answer
The controlled document set should include an account register, source-system interface register, intake form, item register, evidence index, matching-decision record, aging and escalation schedule, journal request and approval record, posting-status log, reconciliation worksheet, closure checklist, exception register, root-cause tracker, and management reporting schedule. Each record should have a stable identifier, defined owner, required fields, applicable status values, source references, and retention rule. Forms should distinguish mandatory information from optional information and define what is required before a workflow step can proceed. The forms must align with the actual organization's account structure, systems, approval limits, and source-process procedures. Generic templates should be tested using representative transactions before adoption. Version history and controlled document inventories should make it possible to identify which approved form and policy version governed each transaction.

Study instruction:
- "Inventory the current registers and forms, map each one to its control objective, and identify missing owners, evidence fields, approval steps, and closure criteria."
Key point:
- Forms should support specific decisions and controls rather than create paperwork without purpose.
- Controlled versions and defined fields improve consistency and auditability.
47. Q: How should the organization manage changes to the suspense and clearing SOP, its parameters, and related system configuration?
A: Show answer
Every proposed change should identify the reason, affected account types, process steps, matching rules, required evidence, system routes, roles, and reports. Parameter changes such as match tolerance, date window, aging profile, delegation limit, exchange-rate source, reconciliation frequency, retention schedule, and retry behavior should be recorded with a value, unit, owner, approval, and effective date. Where a value or rule has not been approved, the process must not silently apply an arbitrary default that enables clearance. Changes should be tested on representative positive and negative cases, reviewed independently, and deployed using an authorized change process. Users should be informed and trained where their responsibilities change. Earlier versions and affected transaction histories must remain available so historical decisions can be reconstructed under the rules that applied at the time.

Study instruction:
- "Select a proposed parameter change and document its approval, impact assessment, testing, effective date, communication, and rollback or remediation requirements."
Key point:
- Parameters affecting financial decisions require controlled approval and testing.
- Unapproved configuration values must not authorize operational clearance.
48. Q: How should the organization validate that a newly designed suspense-control SOP reflects actual operations before adopting it?
A: Show answer
Conduct a walkthrough of one or more completed periods using real approved records from source transactions through classification, investigation, matching, journal approval, posting, reconciliation, and closure. Compare actual actions with the proposed procedure, and record who performed each step, which evidence exists, and how system outcomes were confirmed. Test normal transactions and difficult cases, such as incomplete source records, duplicates, partial settlements, interface failures, and legacy balances. Gaps should identify the risk, required policy decision, control owner, corrective action, and target date. Distinguish evidence observed from verbal explanations or assumptions. The implementation review should also verify document completeness, account applicability, source references, configuration restrictions, and actual authorization limits. A fictional demonstration or successful document-generation test does not establish that the organization has adopted or operationalized the procedure.

Study instruction:
- "Perform an end-to-end walkthrough and produce an evidence-backed gap register distinguishing verified practices, unverified assumptions, and changes required before approval."
Key point:
- SOP design must be tested against actual transaction evidence and operating procedures.
- Demonstration outputs and internal tests do not prove organizational adoption or production effectiveness.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger process walkthroughs
49. Q: How should a suspense-account SOP be approved and made effective for an actual NGO?
A: Show answer
Before adoption, the organization should verify the legal entity, jurisdiction, reporting framework, accounting policy, account register, source-system routes, delegated authorities, control parameters, and relevant contractual or donor requirements. Responsible finance, system, legal, tax, grants, and management owners should review the sections applicable to their duties. The SOP must identify its owner, version, approval authority, effective date, review date, scope, and controlled repository. Implementation should include staff training, system changes, test evidence, approved registers, reporting arrangements, and a defined procedure for exceptions and handover. Any unresolved critical gate should remain visible and prevent the affected function from being treated as operationally approved. A signed draft does not substitute for actual configuration or effective control operation; adoption must be evidenced and the implementation periodically reassessed.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare an adoption checklist and identify the approvals, system evidence, training records, and unresolved critical issues required before implementation."
Key point:
- Actual adoption requires verified authority, approved policy, effective controls, and implementation evidence.
- Unresolved critical requirements should not be relabeled as complete merely to release the document.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger processes and reporting responsibilities
50. Q: How can an organization determine whether suspense and clearing controls remain effective after implementation?
A: Show answer
Effectiveness monitoring should combine recurring reconciliations, sample testing, aging reviews, interface exception analysis, access reviews, journal approval checks, closure-evidence inspections, and follow-up of root-cause actions. The reviewer should confirm not only that documented procedures exist, but also that staff follow them and the systems enforce required restrictions. Management should examine exceptions that recur despite prior remediation, changes in open-item profiles, unexplained register-to-GL differences, premature closures, unsupported write-offs, duplicate postings, and stale or bypassed approvals. Control failures should be assigned to accountable owners with target dates and independent retesting. The monitoring schedule should specify frequency, population, testing method, escalation criteria, reviewer, evidence retained, and resolution requirements. Effectiveness conclusions must be grounded in the period and population actually tested, not generalized from a small sample without justification.

Study instruction:
- "Design a periodic control-testing schedule and perform sample tests covering authorization, matching, posting verification, reconciliation, closure, and corrective-action effectiveness."
Key point:
- Control effectiveness depends on actual operation, not documentation alone.
- Repeated exceptions require investigation and independent retesting of corrective actions.
Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger reconciliation and closing controls
