Table 8
Interfund, Interproject, and Interoffice Reconciliation
1. Q: What is the primary purpose of an interfund, interproject, and interoffice reconciliation procedure?
A: Show answer
The primary purpose is to establish a controlled, evidence-based process for reconciling reciprocal transactions and balances between funding pools, projects, grants, field offices, headquarters, and approved related entities. The process must demonstrate that both sides have included the relevant transactions, that opening balances and current-period movements explain closing balances, and that differences are investigated rather than concealed by aggregate agreement. It also establishes responsibilities, independent review, counterpart confirmation, correction approval, escalation, and audit-trail requirements. A mathematical match alone does not prove completeness, correct classification, donor eligibility, valid authorization, or compliance with the applicable accounting framework. The supplied SOP is explicitly a fictional demonstration, not an approved organizational policy. Actual adoption requires verification of the entity structure, accounting framework, contractual terms, donor conditions, internal policies, system configuration, and authorized approvals.

Study instruction:
- "Explain the difference between mathematical agreement and a completed, evidence-supported reconciliation."

Key point:
- Reconciliation establishes completeness, accuracy, classification, and traceability, not merely equal totals.
- The example SOP must not be used as an adopted procedure without authorized review.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger
2. Q: What activities should be covered by the reconciliation SOP, and which should remain outside its scope?
A: Show answer
The SOP should govern relationship identification, source-data extraction, completeness testing, normalization, transaction matching, opening-to-closing balance calculations, difference investigation, counterpart confirmation, independent review, exception monitoring, and verification of corrections. Activities such as payment authorization, bank reconciliation, donor setup, journal execution, accounts payable and receivable subledger controls, partner liquidation, suspense-account clearance, tax filing, consolidation adjustments, and donor-report preparation may belong to other procedures. The distinction matters because reconciliation can identify a problem without having the authority to resolve every underlying issue. For example, a disputed vendor charge may require an accounts payable correction, while a donor eligibility question requires review of the applicable agreement. Each referral must have a unique reference, receiving process, accountable owner, acknowledgement deadline, target resolution date, linked reconciliation item, and required closure evidence. Referring an issue does not automatically close the associated reconciliation.

Study instruction:
- "Map each activity in the reconciliation cycle to its responsible process and document how an unresolved item is transferred and followed up."

Key point:
- Every process must have a clearly defined owner and decision-making authority.
- Referral does not remove an unresolved exception from reconciliation monitoring.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger processes
3. Q: How should interfund, interproject, interoffice, and inter-entity relationships be distinguished?
A: Show answer
An interfund relationship concerns funding pools within an organizational accounting structure, subject to the applicable legal, accounting, and donor restrictions. An interproject relationship concerns charges, allocations, recharges, or clearing balances between projects or grants. An interoffice relationship concerns reciprocal accounting between headquarters, field offices, or regional offices. An inter-entity relationship concerns transactions between legally distinct entities maintaining separate books. These categories are not interchangeable: two offices may belong to the same legal entity, whereas two affiliated organizations may have separate financial statements and legal obligations. Classification determines which ledgers, counterparties, agreements, reporting dimensions, and approvals are relevant. The organization must verify its actual legal structure and reporting perimeter rather than infer legal relationships from office names or account descriptions. Consolidation and elimination, where required, must follow the applicable framework and remain distinct from routine transaction matching.

Study instruction:
- "Classify representative transactions into the four relationship types and identify the supporting legal, accounting, and contractual evidence."

Key point:
- Relationship classification determines the appropriate reconciliation scope and approval route.
- Similar transaction descriptions do not establish that two balances belong to the same legal or reporting perimeter.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and reporting structure
4. Q: What is the difference between transaction matching, balance reconciliation, and a timing difference?
A: Show answer
Transaction matching links individual accounting records across the two sides of a relationship. Balance reconciliation tests whether complete signed balances, opening balances, and current-period movements are mutually explained. A timing difference arises when a transaction has been recorded on one side but the corresponding record is expected to appear on the other side in a different period, supported by evidence and an expected clearance date. These are separate tests. Matching individual transactions does not automatically prove that all transactions were extracted, and two closing balances that offset do not prove that individual charges were classified correctly. A timing difference must also remain visible until subsequent-period evidence confirms its clearance. The reconciliation should record each result separately, including matched items, unmatched items, documented timing differences, classification disputes, foreign-exchange differences, and unresolved confirmation requirements.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a transaction match register and a separate balance bridge, then identify which differences qualify as supported timing items."

Key point:
- Transaction-level matching and balance-level reconciliation answer different control questions.
- Timing differences require evidence, an owner, a target date, and subsequent-period verification.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and open-item clearing
5. Q: Which accounting framework, organizational policies, contracts, and donor requirements should govern a reconciliation decision?
A: Show answer
The procedure must identify the applicable legal requirements, accounting framework, approved finance policies, executed donor or grant agreements, relevant contractual terms, and authorized relationship mappings. The controlling requirements may vary according to the actual entity, transaction, fund, reporting perimeter, and effective period. A software configuration, historical practice, matching algorithm, informal email, or equal reciprocal balance cannot independently authorize accounting treatment or expenditure. Maintain an authority register recording the source issuer, document title or identifier, applicable provision, version, effective period, date checked, applicability rationale, reviewer, and change trigger. Existence of a regulation or accounting standard is not equivalent to confirmation that a particular requirement applies to the organization. When the applicable framework or contractual authority is unknown or conflicting, place the dependent decision on hold and refer it to the competent Finance, Legal, Tax, Compliance, or Grants owner.

Study instruction:
- "Trace a reconciliation decision to its applicable framework, policy or agreement and document how applicability was established."

Key point:
- Applicable authority must be verified before making context-dependent accounting or donor decisions.
- Unresolved authority questions require a documented hold and escalation, not an assumed treatment.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger accounting processes
6. Q: How should the legal-entity and reporting perimeter be verified before reconciliation begins?
A: Show answer
Establish which legal entities, accounting ledgers, reporting units, offices, funds, projects, grants, and counterparties are included in the reconciliation. Confirm whether the transaction represents movement within a single entity, activity between distinct entities, or a relationship that belongs to a separate control process. Record the responsible accounting owners, applicable reporting currency, relevant dimensions, transaction direction, and whether counterpart confirmation or a separate consolidation workpaper is required. Where legal-entity status is uncertain, do not infer it from organizational charts alone; verify the registered structure and authorized internal records. Changes to the organizational structure or reporting perimeter must trigger reassessment of existing relationship mappings and open balances. An incorrect perimeter can cause valid transactions to be excluded, unrelated balances to be netted together, or consolidation adjustments to be incorrectly posted to entity books.

Study instruction:
- "Select an office-to-office and an affiliate transaction, then document the legal entity and reporting perimeter for each."

Key point:
- The reconciliation perimeter must be established before selecting transaction populations.
- Separate-entity accounting and consolidation adjustments require distinct analysis and authorization.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and financial reporting
7. Q: What information should be maintained in the entity and counterparty relationship master?
A: Show answer
The relationship master should identify a unique relationship ID, relationship type, legal entity and ledger on each side, office, fund, project, donor or grant, counterparty identifier, account and dimension mappings, permitted transaction direction, currencies, responsible owners, reconciliation frequency, confirmation requirement, balance sign convention, effective dates, and escalation route. It should also identify the approved source reports and any special conditions that govern matching. Mappings must be effective-dated so transactions are classified using the correct relationship for their transaction date. Historical periods should not silently inherit a new mapping when an organization changes a project code or reporting structure. Changes require approval, a new version, an implementation date, and an impact assessment of open and completed reconciliations. Unverified or missing mappings must be treated as control exceptions rather than silently assigned to a default category.

Study instruction:
- "Design a relationship-master record and test whether it contains enough information to reproduce the scope and rules of a completed reconciliation."

Key point:
- A controlled master record provides a consistent foundation for reconciliation and reporting.
- Mapping changes require effective dates, authorization, and traceable historical treatment.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger master data
8. Q: How should responsibilities and segregation of duties be structured across the reconciliation process?
A: Show answer
Assign distinct responsibilities for source-data preparation, population-completeness verification, reconciliation preparation, difference investigation, counterpart confirmation, correction approval, journal posting, independent review, and final sign-off. The preparer should not be the sole person who validates the results and approves closure. The person posting a correction should not replace the independent reviewer of that correction. Where limited staffing prevents full segregation, management should document a compensating control, such as independent review of source extracts and posting evidence, restricted system access, or a second-person verification of completed work. Define deputy responsibilities, approval limits, escalation authority, and access rights. The responsibility matrix must reflect actual roles and permissions, not merely proposed job titles. External consultants may support reconciliation, but the organization must retain its own responsibility for valid approvals, accounting decisions, and evidence retention.

Study instruction:
- "Create a responsibility matrix for the entire workflow and identify conflicts between preparer, approver, poster, and reviewer roles."

Key point:
- Independent review reduces the risk of errors or inappropriate adjustments remaining undetected.
- Compensating controls require named owners and evidence that the control operated.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger processes and approvals
9. Q: How should the reconciliation calendar and period responsibilities be established?
A: Show answer
Establish a calendar that identifies each relationship, reporting period, source-data deadline, preparer, reviewer, counterpart confirmation deadline, target investigation date, escalation milestone, approval deadline, and final archive date. Frequency should follow the risk, transaction volume, reporting obligations, organizational policy, and materiality of the relationship. High-risk or high-volume relationships may require more frequent monitoring than stable low-volume relationships. Internal deadlines should leave sufficient time for data correction, counterpart follow-up, and approval before financial reporting deadlines. Calendar changes should be authorized and communicated to all relevant owners. Track the status of each relationship as not started, awaiting data, in progress, under investigation, awaiting confirmation, pending correction, reviewed with exceptions, reconciled without exceptions, or formally held. Completion status must reflect evidence rather than a preparer's estimate.

Study instruction:
- "Build a monthly reconciliation calendar with owners, internal deadlines, escalation points, and evidence requirements."

Key point:
- Scheduling should be risk-based and integrated with the financial close.
- A calendar is a control tracker, not proof that the underlying reconciliation is complete.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger closing processes
10. Q: How can finance demonstrate that the source transaction population is complete on both sides?
A: Show answer
Obtain the relevant source reports or ledger extracts independently for each side of the relationship and preserve the extraction date, period, entity, ledger, account, fund, project, counterparty, status filters, row count, and total signed value. Record the report name, source system, extraction parameters, preparer, file version, and evidence reference. Compare the population against expected accounts and transaction classes, reconcile control totals to the source system, and investigate missing or unexpected records. Where applicable, include posted, open, cleared, reversed, and relevant adjustment items according to the defined scope. A spreadsheet prepared manually without evidence of the underlying system population is not sufficient proof of completeness. If one side's data is missing or its query has not been validated, the reconciliation should remain incomplete or on hold rather than being classified as a valid zero-difference result.

Study instruction:
- "Reproduce both sides' source reports and compare their parameters, record counts, totals, and included accounting statuses."

Key point:
- Complete and independently reproducible source populations are prerequisites for reliable reconciliation.
- Missing data or unexplained control-total differences must be resolved before sign-off.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger reports and balances
11. Q: Which fields should be standardized before transaction records are matched?
A: Show answer
Standardize transaction date, document date, posting date, period, reference number, document type, account, amount, currency, signed amount, counterparty, legal entity, office, fund, project, donor or grant, and source-record identifier. Normalize date formats, text spacing, leading zeros, currency codes, sign conventions, and approved aliases without deleting the original values. Preserve both source fields and normalized fields so reviewers can reproduce the transformation. Mapping should be explicit, controlled, and effective-dated. Do not normalize away meaningful differences such as distinct legal entities, fund restrictions, or project dimensions merely because the amounts are equal. Each transformation should produce a traceable output and a record of rejected or ambiguous records. This makes the reconciliation repeatable across source systems without allowing data-cleaning logic to change the underlying accounting meaning.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a crosswalk of the source fields and normalized fields, then test it on transactions with inconsistent dates, references, and dimensions."

Key point:
- Standardization supports reliable matching while preserving original evidence.
- Meaningful accounting dimensions must never be erased merely to improve match rates.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger master data and account records
12. Q: What matching rules should be used for reciprocal transaction reconciliation?
A: Show answer
Define a hierarchy of approved matching rules. An exact match may require an agreed reference, compatible dimensions, opposite signed amounts, compatible currencies, and an acceptable date relationship. A grouped match may link one record on one side to several records on the other where documentary evidence supports a legitimate batch or allocation. Foreign-currency matching requires a controlled comparison of source currency, conversion basis, and reporting-currency values. Every rule must specify its prerequisites, matching key, allowable grouping, exception conditions, evidence requirements, and status produced. The match engine should explain why records matched instead of providing only a result flag. Uncertain candidates should be proposed for review, not accepted automatically. A match must also prevent one source row from being reused across multiple unrelated groups, which could otherwise create false agreement.

Study instruction:
- "Test exact, one-to-many, many-to-one, and foreign-currency matching cases and document the evidence required for each outcome."

Key point:
- Matching rules must be deterministic, authorized, and explainable.
- Complex matches require supporting evidence and controlled exception handling.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and open-item clearing
13. Q: How should duplicate transactions and reuse of source rows be prevented during matching?
A: Show answer
Assign a unique identifier to each source record and each match group. Validate that every source row enters the reconciliation population once and cannot be allocated to several incompatible matches. Use multiple attributes to identify potential duplicates, including document reference, counterparty, amount, date, transaction type, source-system identifier, and accounting dimensions. A repeated reference may be legitimate when different suppliers use the same number, while one real transaction may appear in multiple systems under different identifiers. Therefore, duplicate detection should produce candidates for investigation rather than automatically delete records based on one field. Track record counts and signed totals before and after transformations, and confirm that grouped matching conserves the amounts of the source records. Any exclusion, reversal, or duplicate disposition requires a documented reason and approval where required.

Study instruction:
- "Create a test population with duplicated references, genuine repeated amounts, and reused match candidates, then verify that the control detects anomalies without deleting valid records."

Key point:
- No source record should be silently omitted or reused in incompatible match groups.
- Candidate duplicates require verification against original evidence.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Open-item management and general ledger records
14. Q: Why is an aggregate balance match insufficient when fund, grant, project, or counterparty dimensions differ?
A: Show answer
Aggregate agreement can conceal a misclassification, an unauthorized allocation, a missing transaction, or unrelated amounts that happen to offset. For example, a charge of IDR 1,000,000 recorded against an unrestricted fund on one side and a restricted fund on the other may have equal and opposite values but remain incorrectly classified. The same problem arises when two unrelated counterparties have balances of equal magnitude. Reconciliation must therefore test both amounts and the dimensions that define the approved relationship. A total-level difference may also disappear when one error offsets another. Preserve disputed records individually and investigate the supporting agreements, transaction purpose, dimensions, and posting evidence. Do not approve the relationship merely because the net residual is zero.

Study instruction:
- "Review a reconciliation with equal aggregate totals but mismatched fund or project codes and demonstrate why it must remain unresolved."

Key point:
- Correct dimensions and transaction identity are essential even when total balances agree.
- Offsetting errors must not be netted into an unsupported closure decision.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — Chart of accounts and General Ledger master data
15. Q: How should the opening-to-closing balance bridge be calculated and verified?
A: Show answer
For each side, calculate the closing balance as the opening balance plus the sum of signed movements during the period, adjusted only for explicitly identified and supported items according to the approved bridge design. The relationship record should show opening balance, movement population, adjustments where permitted, computed closing balance, reported closing balance, and residual. Reperform the calculation independently and reconcile the values to source-system reports. Investigate differences caused by missing records, incorrect period filters, reversals, duplicated rows, inconsistent signs, or unsupported adjustments. Never alter the opening balance solely to make the current period tie. Where an approved prior-period correction is necessary, record its authority, accounting treatment, posting evidence, and effect on comparability. The bridge must be reproducible from preserved inputs and calculation logic.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate opening plus signed movements for both sides and reconcile the results to the reported closing balances."

Key point:
- The bridge explains how each closing balance arose from the opening balance and period movements.
- Unsupported plugs and arbitrary changes to opening balances undermine the audit trail.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger balances and trial balance reports
16. Q: How should signed balances be used when comparing reciprocal accounts?
A: Show answer
Establish a sign convention for every relationship type and apply it consistently. If one side is represented as positive and its reciprocal is represented as negative, the signed residual may be calculated by adding the two appropriately classified balances. For instance, IDR 5,000,000 on one side and negative IDR 5,000,000 on the other produce a zero signed residual, subject to all other controls being satisfied. Absolute values must not be compared in isolation because they conceal directional errors and can make two balances appear equal even when their accounting signs are wrong. Foreign-currency values should retain source currency and amount alongside the reporting-currency amount. Reconciliation templates must document the applicable sign convention, test for unexpected signs, and retain the individual source values needed to reproduce each calculation.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate reciprocal balances using signed amounts and test the result against a case containing an incorrect debit or credit direction."

Key point:
- Signed balances preserve the direction of reciprocal accounting entries.
- Equal absolute magnitudes do not establish a correct reciprocal relationship.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger balances and postings
17. Q: How should reconciliation differences be classified and investigated?
A: Show answer
Use a controlled classification such as timing difference, missing counterpart posting, incorrect amount, incorrect fund or project dimension, duplicate transaction, incorrect account mapping, foreign-exchange difference, incomplete source population, unconfirmed related-entity balance, unsupported allocation, or suspected unauthorized activity. Each difference should have a unique exception ID, relationship ID, period, original records, amount, currency, dimensions, evidence references, risk level, accountable owner, target date, current status, proposed action, and closure criteria. Classification should describe the observed issue without assuming its cause. An unexplained difference must not be labelled a timing item simply because the parties expect it to resolve later. Investigation should identify what happened, which record is authoritative, whether a correction is required, who can authorize it, and how the result will be verified.

Study instruction:
- "Classify a sample of unmatched transactions and state the evidence needed to confirm the cause and determine the corrective action."

Key point:
- A clear exception taxonomy supports consistent investigation and reporting.
- Differences remain unresolved until the evidence and authorized disposition satisfy the closure criteria.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and correction workflows
18. Q: What evidence is required before an exception can be accepted as resolved?
A: Show answer
Evidence should directly support the conclusion being made. Depending on the issue, it may include an original invoice, approved recharge calculation, signed agreement, source ledger extract, journal document, correction authorization, actual posting confirmation, independently verified counterpart response, exchange-rate source, or subsequent-period receipt record. A narrative explanation alone is not equivalent to source evidence. Record the evidence ID, document location, custodian, date, source system, relevant transaction or amount, and the assertion it supports. Distinguish evidence that a request was sent from evidence that the counterparty received and confirmed it. Likewise, an approved correction request does not demonstrate that the correction was posted. Evidence must be reviewed for completeness, authenticity, relevance, and consistency with other records before the corresponding exception is closed.

Study instruction:
- "Choose three exception types and define the minimum evidence that would establish their cause, approval, posting, and final resolution."

Key point:
- Evidence must substantiate the specific conclusion and control performed.
- Requests, explanations, approvals, and completed postings are distinct evidence events.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and document traceability
19. Q: How should exception ageing and risk-based escalation operate?
A: Show answer
Calculate the age of each unresolved item from a defined and consistently applied starting date, such as the date first identified or the date an agreed action became overdue. The selected convention must be documented so periods can be compared meaningfully. Prioritize exceptions according to financial exposure, donor or legal restrictions, potential misstatement, reporting deadline, recurrence, evidence quality, and risk of unauthorized activity, rather than age alone. Assign an accountable owner, next action, target date, and escalation level. Overdue items should trigger reminders and, at defined thresholds, referral to management or the relevant specialist. Record the escalation date, recipient, response, revised deadline, and decision. Repeated extensions must not become a substitute for resolution; significant items may require a qualified reconciliation status or hold on dependent reporting.

Study instruction:
- "Create an aged-exception report that ranks items using risk, value, deadline, recurrence, and time outstanding."

Key point:
- Ageing makes unresolved differences visible and supports timely intervention.
- Risk-based escalation is more effective than prioritizing exceptions only by age or amount.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger control processes
20. Q: Why is counterpart confirmation required even when reciprocal balances agree mathematically?
A: Show answer
Counterpart confirmation provides independent evidence that the other party recognizes the relevant balance, transaction, and period. It can reveal differences in classification, missing postings, disputed charges, or incorrect assumptions that are invisible in an internally generated comparison. A confirmation process should identify the appropriate counterparty, verify that the contact is authorized, specify the period and balance being confirmed, preserve the request and supporting schedule, and record the response or non-response. Confirmation requirements should follow the relationship's risk, the applicable policy, and the nature of the relationship. For legally distinct entities, reciprocal arithmetic agreement is not by itself proof that the transaction is valid or that the reporting requirements have been satisfied. The SOP's fictional affiliate case expressly retains a hold while independent confirmation remains outstanding.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a counterpart confirmation request and document the difference between evidence of delivery, a response, and an independently verified confirmation."

Key point:
- Independent confirmation strengthens the evidence supporting reciprocal balances.
- Arithmetic agreement does not replace a required confirmation or authorized review.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and reconciliation processes
21. Q: How should an unresponsive or disputing counterparty be handled?
A: Show answer
Maintain a confirmation log recording the request ID, counterparty, verified contact, request date, amount and period, supporting documents, delivery status, response status, follow-up date, and accountable owner. Send follow-ups through the approved communication channel and escalate persistent non-response according to the relationship's risk and reporting deadlines. For a disputed amount, request the specific transaction records, dimensions, approvals, and calculation that explain the disagreement. Do not treat silence as agreement unless an applicable, authorized policy expressly permits that conclusion and all required conditions are met. Where confirmation is mandatory, preserve the unresolved status until the required evidence or formally authorized disposition is obtained. If the deadline is approaching, management should determine the appropriate reporting qualification or hold instead of allowing the preparer to close the item without approval.

Study instruction:
- "Simulate a counterparty that fails to respond and another that disputes a dimension, then apply the documented follow-up and escalation rules."

Key point:
- Non-response and substantive disagreement are separate exception states.
- Neither should be converted to confirmation merely to complete the reconciliation calendar.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger controls
22. Q: What should an independent reviewer examine before signing off a reconciliation?
A: Show answer
The reviewer should verify the relationship scope, legal-entity mapping, applicable period, source extraction parameters, population completeness, opening balances, movement totals, closing balances, signed residual calculations, matching rules, dimensions, duplicates, difference classification, counterpart evidence, and outstanding exceptions. The reviewer should also assess whether any accounting decision depends on an unverified policy, contractual term, donor restriction, or external requirement. Significant manual adjustments, unusual matches, changes to mappings, foreign-currency conversions, and corrections after approval require focused review. Record the reviewer's name, date, reconciliation version, evidence examined, questions raised, required rework, and final decision. A preparer cannot close a review point merely by changing the status label; the reviewer needs evidence that the issue has been resolved or an authorized qualification has been accepted.

Study instruction:
- "Use a reviewer checklist on a completed reconciliation and document how each material assertion is supported."

Key point:
- Independent review evaluates the underlying evidence and logic, not just the final residual.
- Sign-off must identify the version reviewed and distinguish clean completion from qualified or held status.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and journal workflows
23. Q: How should an accounting correction be distinguished from a referral to another process?
A: Show answer
A correction is appropriate when evidence establishes that an accounting record or approved accounting dimension is wrong and the competent owner authorizes the necessary change. A referral is appropriate when the issue belongs to another process or requires a decision that the reconciliation team does not have authority to make. For example, a misposted fund dimension may require an accounting correction, whereas a questionable donor cost allocation may require review of the executed agreement and approval from the responsible Grants or Compliance owner. The exception register should link both the reconciliation ID and the correction or referral ID, state the reason, identify the authorized decision-maker, and specify required evidence. Do not create a balancing entry simply to eliminate an unexplained residual. Reconciliation status must remain appropriately qualified until the source issue has been resolved or a valid authorized disposition has been documented.

Study instruction:
- "Classify sample issues into correction, referral, or additional investigation and identify who has the authority to decide each case."

Key point:
- Reconciliation identifies and controls differences but does not automatically authorize their accounting treatment.
- Referrals must remain traceable until the responsible process returns evidence or a valid disposition.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger entries and corrections
24. Q: What evidence is necessary to verify that an approved correction has actually been posted?
A: Show answer
Retain the correction request, documented reason, supporting evidence, approved accounting treatment, authorized approver, posting reference, source-system document number, effective accounting period, and evidence of the actual posted result. The person confirming the correction should independently compare the posting against the approval, including account, dimensions, amount, currency, entity, and period. Then rerun the affected matching and balance-bridge calculations. A correction can resolve one difference without resolving other exceptions, so the rerun must preserve unrelated open items. If the entry was rejected, reversed incorrectly, posted to the wrong period, or left in a parked state, the correction must not be marked complete. Link the old and new records so a reviewer can reconstruct what was changed, why it was changed, and how the resulting balance was verified.

Study instruction:
- "Trace one approved correction from request to actual posting and rerun the affected reconciliation using the resulting source records."

Key point:
- Approval, posting, and successful post-correction verification are separate control stages.
- Closure requires actual posting evidence and a verified rerun, not a completed request form alone.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger document processing
25. Q: How should foreign-currency reciprocal transactions be reconciled?
A: Show answer
Preserve the original currency and amount, the reported currency amount on each side, the conversion rate, the source of that rate, the relevant date, the conversion direction, and the approved method. First investigate whether the transactions are genuinely reciprocal in their underlying currency and purpose; then determine whether reporting-currency differences arise from valid conversion requirements, inconsistent rates, incorrect dates, calculation errors, or missing information. The appropriate recognition and measurement treatment depends on the applicable accounting framework and organizational policy. The SOP should not prescribe a rate merely because it produces matching balances. Any unresolved rate or accounting-policy question should remain on hold pending a decision by the authorized financial-accounting owner. Retain the supporting rate evidence and independently recalculate the conversion before accepting the result.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate a foreign-currency reciprocal item using the approved rate source and compare both source-currency and reporting-currency values."

Key point:
- Currency reconciliation must preserve both original amounts and their reporting-currency conversions.
- Rate differences require evidence and an applicable policy decision, not arbitrary balancing adjustments.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger accounting and reporting processes
26. Q: How should shared-cost recharges and interproject allocations be validated?
A: Show answer
Verify the executed agreement, eligible cost pool, allocation purpose, authorized receiving projects, approved allocation driver, source transaction population, calculation period, and applicable approval. The driver might be a documented measure such as eligible headcount or another appropriate allocation basis, but the example SOP's fictional driver must not be treated as an actual organizational requirement. Recalculate the distribution independently, test that allocated amounts reconcile to the approved pool, and document any residual from rounding. Inspect whether the same underlying cost has already been charged to another project, reimbursed by another source, or included more than once. Validate each recipient's project and fund dimensions as well as the total. A mathematically correct allocation is not sufficient if the donor terms prohibit the cost or the allocation basis lacks authority. Disputed eligibility should be referred to the competent owner before final approval.

Study instruction:
- "Recalculate a shared-cost allocation, verify its source pool and driver, and perform a double-funding check across all recipient projects."

Key point:
- Allocation calculations must be supported by an approved basis and eligible recipient scope.
- Mathematical accuracy does not establish donor eligibility or permission to recharge.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and cost-allocation interfaces
27. Q: How should donor restrictions and fund classifications affect interfund reconciliation?
A: Show answer
Confirm that each transaction is recorded against the correct funding pool, grant, donor, project, and organizational dimensions, and verify that the proposed movement is permitted by the applicable agreement and approved internal policy. A transfer between funding pools within a legal entity does not automatically create external income or authorize a reallocation of restricted resources. Similarly, a balanced journal does not demonstrate that the receiving project is eligible to absorb the charge. Retain the source agreement, approval, calculation, classification rationale, and evidence of both ledger postings. When eligibility or restriction scope is uncertain, do not infer approval from a previous similar transaction. Refer the issue to the Grants, Finance, Legal, or Compliance authority designated by the organization. Any resulting accounting entry must follow the applicable framework and policy rather than a generic reconciliation rule.

Study instruction:
- "Trace a cross-project recharge to its grant terms, approved allocation, fund coding, and reciprocal postings."

Key point:
- Funding restrictions and accounting classification must be tested in addition to reciprocal amounts.
- Reconciliation does not authorize a donor-funded movement or reclassification by itself.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and grants-management interfaces
28. Q: How should balances between related legal entities be handled differently from interoffice balances within one legal entity?
A: Show answer
First confirm the legal entities and reporting perimeter. For offices within one legal entity, reciprocal balances may relate to internal ledger dimensions or clearing arrangements, depending on the accounting design. For legally distinct entities, each entity maintains its own accounting records and requires evidence supporting its own recognition, classification, and presentation. Any consolidation elimination or group reporting adjustment must be prepared in the designated consolidation process, with separate authority and evidence; it should not be posted automatically to either entity's books merely to produce a consolidated match. Counterpart confirmation and valid relationship mappings may be essential for inter-entity balances. If both entity books agree but required confirmation remains outstanding, maintain the unresolved status. A shared parent, common donor, or similar account description does not remove the need to establish the legal relationship and reporting treatment.

Study instruction:
- "Compare one same-entity interoffice balance with one separate-entity balance and identify which approvals and reporting steps differ."

Key point:
- Legal-entity boundaries influence accounting, confirmation, and consolidation procedures.
- Consolidation entries require their own workpaper and approval process.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and financial reporting
29. Q: How should cut-off and transactions posted around the end of a reporting period be handled?
A: Show answer
Identify the appropriate accounting period using the applicable accounting policy and underlying transaction facts. Compare transaction dates, document dates, posting dates, service or transfer dates, approval dates, and expected counterpart posting dates. A transaction posted on one side before period-end and on the other side after period-end may be a legitimate timing difference, but that conclusion requires documentary support. Late postings, reversals, backdated records, and transactions recorded in the wrong period should be evaluated according to the applicable policy and materiality requirements. Record the original period, discovery date, expected clearance date, accountable owner, and any reporting implications. Do not move a difference into the next period merely to remove it from the current exception register. Subsequent-period evidence should be linked to the original item while preserving the historical status of the earlier reconciliation.

Study instruction:
- "Review transactions immediately before and after period-end, then distinguish a supported timing item from a genuine cut-off error."

Key point:
- Cut-off decisions require evidence about the transaction and the applicable accounting policy.
- Subsequent-period clearance should not erase the historical reconciliation trail.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger closing and reporting
30. Q: What is the proper treatment of a transfer recorded by one office but received by the other office in a subsequent period?
A: Show answer
Establish that the original transfer was authorized through the appropriate source process and verify the sending-side entry, transaction date, amount, destination, and supporting evidence. The receiving side should be checked for a corresponding posting in the current or subsequent period. Until that posting is verified, maintain the item as an unresolved timing difference or transfer in transit according to the approved classification. Assign an accountable owner and expected clearance date. When the receiving-side entry appears, match it to the original item, preserve the subsequent-period evidence, update the roll-forward, and obtain the required review. The example SOP includes a fictional IDR 2,500,000 remittance recorded by headquarters in September and cleared by the Jakarta office in October. This illustrates the method only; it is not evidence of an actual transfer or a mandatory accounting treatment for every case.

Study instruction:
- "Trace a transfer-in-transit item from its sending-side evidence to the subsequent-period receipt and the updated reconciliation."

Key point:
- Timing differences require an identifiable originating transaction and verified subsequent clearance.
- A later receipt should be linked to, not substituted for, the original period's exception record.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and closing processes
31. Q: How can a reconciliation be classified as having nil activity without relying on an empty spreadsheet?
A: Show answer
Verify that the relationship was expected to exist during the period, all relevant source systems and accounts were queried, the entity and counterparty filters were correct, the transaction classes were complete, and the queries returned zero relevant records. Preserve both source reports, the selection criteria, row counts, total amounts, extraction dates, attestation by the responsible owner, and independent review. Check for alternative references or coding patterns that could cause transactions to escape the original query. A blank register alone is not evidence of nil activity; it may indicate that data were never imported. If the relationship was not active or was out of scope, record that status separately from an attested zero population. Only classify the reconciliation as nil activity when the evidence supports the conclusion and the required approvals are complete.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a nil-activity certification from actual source-query results, record the filters and control totals, and obtain independent review."

Key point:
- Nil activity is a verified result of a complete search, not an absence of data entry.
- Source-query evidence and independent attestation are necessary to support a zero-activity conclusion.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger reports
32. Q: How should recurring reconciliations carry forward opening balances and unresolved differences into the next period?
A: Show answer
Use a controlled carry-forward that preserves the prior-period closing balance, unresolved exception IDs, original amounts, supporting evidence, responsible owners, expected clearance dates, and historical status. Reconcile the carried-forward values to the new period's opening balances rather than overwriting them with the latest figures. Cleared timing items should retain a link to the actual subsequent posting, while persistent classification disputes or unexplained residuals should remain visible. If a prior-period correction changes the opening balance, document the authorization, posting evidence, effective period, and consequences for the comparative reconciliation. Carry-forward should distinguish new differences from older items so management can identify repeated failures. The process must not automatically classify a carried item as resolved merely because it appears in a new reporting period.

Study instruction:
- "Roll forward one completed period and verify that all opening balances and unresolved exception references remain traceable."

Key point:
- Carry-forward preserves the history and accountability of unresolved balances.
- Prior-period changes require controlled authorization and a documented impact assessment.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger balances and period-end activities
33. Q: When should a completed reconciliation be reopened after sign-off?
A: Show answer
Reopening may be necessary when new source transactions are identified, the source population proves incomplete, a material classification error is discovered, an unauthorized mapping change is identified, a counterparty disputes an accepted balance, or a posted correction changes the reconciled figures. The request should state the original reconciliation ID and version, reason for reopening, affected accounts and dimensions, estimated impact, supporting evidence, requested access or change, authorizer, and review requirements. Preserve the approved original snapshot and create a traceable revised version. Assess whether reopening affects the trial balance, financial statements, project reporting, donor reporting, or another completed process. Restrict reopening authority and retain the record of the decision. A revised reconciliation should clearly show the corrected status and the relationship between the original and replacement versions.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a reopening request for a late-discovered source record and document the impact assessment, approval, and revised sign-off."

Key point:
- Reopening requires a controlled reason, authority, and impact assessment.
- An original approved version should remain available for audit and reconstruction.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger reporting and adjustments
34. Q: Which controls should protect the reconciliation audit trail, source documents, and version history?
A: Show answer
Retain the source extracts, relationship mappings, query parameters, transformed data, match decisions, balance calculations, exception register, confirmation correspondence, approvals, correction postings, reviewer comments, final reports, and archive references. Each record should have a unique identifier and sufficient metadata to establish who created or changed it and when. Preserve original source values alongside normalized outputs. Restrict editing rights for approved records and retain snapshots or controlled version history so changes after sign-off can be reconstructed. Evidence indexes should identify the custodian and stable location of each document. Backups and archive-retrieval processes should be tested. Retention periods must follow the actual legal, donor, contractual, and organizational requirements; the fictional example does not establish a universal retention period. Never replace an earlier approved record without preserving its history.

Study instruction:
- "Reconstruct one reconciliation from archived inputs, calculation results, approvals, corrections, and final sign-off using its evidence index."

Key point:
- A complete audit trail enables independent reproduction of the reconciliation result.
- Version control and evidence retention must support both historical review and current access controls.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and document workflows
35. Q: How should confidential financial data and system access be managed during reconciliation?
A: Show answer
Limit access to source extracts, counterpart records, financial reports, and supporting documents according to each person's assigned responsibilities. Maintain an authorized-user register showing role, permissions, approval, effective date, and access-removal date. Review access when staff transfer, leave, or change responsibilities, and periodically verify whether permissions remain appropriate. Where source files contain personal or confidential information, use controlled storage and approved transmission methods, and avoid distributing unnecessary sensitive fields. Shared credentials and untracked copies undermine accountability. Technical logs should identify file refreshes, manual changes, exports, and other material events where the system supports them. Privacy, security, and retention requirements must be determined from the organization's applicable policies and law. Suspected unauthorized access or manipulation should be escalated through the approved incident-response process.

Study instruction:
- "Review a sample access register and test whether permissions, departures, confidential documents, and material data changes are controlled."

Key point:
- Access should follow least-privilege principles and assigned responsibilities.
- Data confidentiality and traceability are part of reconciliation control, not separate administrative concerns.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and controlled workflows
36. Q: What information should management receive from a reconciliation report?
A: Show answer
The report should summarize the number and value of relationships in scope, completion status, total unmatched value, differences by category, age of unresolved items, material dimensional disputes, foreign-exchange discrepancies, outstanding confirmations, overdue corrections, and matters awaiting specialist decisions. It should distinguish completed reconciliations from qualified reconciliations, held items, and nil-activity attestations. Show the accountable owner, next action, target date, and risk or reporting consequence for each significant exception. Where useful, compare current results with prior periods to highlight recurring errors and worsening trends. Definitions for status, ageing, materiality, and completion rates must be consistent and documented. Automated metrics should be validated against the underlying evidence and source records before management receives the report. The objective is to support decisions and intervention, not merely display totals.

Study instruction:
- "Build a reconciliation dashboard and verify its summary figures against the detailed match groups and exception register."

Key point:
- Management reporting should show both reconciliation status and the risks remaining unresolved.
- KPI and dashboard figures must be reproducible from controlled source data.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger reporting
37. Q: How should suspected misconduct, prohibited transfers, or potentially fraudulent reconciliation activity be handled?
A: Show answer
Treat unexplained manipulation, unauthorized fund transfers, deliberate use of incorrect dimensions, repeated unsupported corrections, suspiciously reused references, altered evidence, or attempts to bypass independent review as potential risk indicators. Preserve original records and logs, restrict unauthorized changes according to policy, and notify the designated Finance, Compliance, Legal, Internal Audit, or other incident-response authority. Do not investigate beyond assigned authority or alter potentially relevant evidence. Record the incident reference, period, relationship, affected records, immediate control actions, assigned investigator, reporting deadlines, and disposition. Routine matching or correction workflows should not be used to conceal, net, or erase suspicious activity. Management should determine whether the matter affects financial reporting, donor obligations, legal requirements, or other procedures. Closure requires a documented authorized disposition and any required follow-up controls.

Study instruction:
- "Develop an escalation scenario involving an unauthorized interfund transfer and specify the evidence-preservation and notification steps."

Key point:
- Suspected misconduct requires controlled escalation rather than routine balancing adjustments.
- Evidence must remain protected and traceable while authorized personnel assess the matter.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger control processes
38. Q: What are the common mistakes that cause an interfund or interoffice reconciliation to produce a misleading result?
A: Show answer
Common mistakes include using incomplete ledger extracts, mixing entities or periods, comparing absolute amounts without checking signs, accepting aggregate equality despite different dimensions, deleting duplicate candidates automatically, reusing records in several match groups, applying unapproved tolerances, treating unconfirmed items as resolved, and accepting correction requests without posting evidence. Other failures include using inconsistent exchange rates, ignoring donor restrictions, changing opening balances to force agreement, and omitting unresolved differences from management reports. These errors often arise because a template or matching algorithm is treated as the decision-maker rather than a tool. Prevent them through controlled source extraction, explicit rules, reconciliation of counts and totals, dimension checks, independent recalculation, documented exceptions, and reviewer sign-off. Negative test cases should deliberately include these failure modes to demonstrate that the procedure flags them rather than producing a false clean result.

Study instruction:
- "Use deliberately incorrect source records to test whether the controls detect incomplete populations, dimensional errors, duplicated matches, and unsupported corrections."

Key point:
- A clean-looking residual can conceal multiple errors or incomplete evidence.
- Effective reconciliation controls must detect adverse and ambiguous cases, not only successful matches.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger controls and open-item management
39. Q: How should a reconciliation be walked through from source records to final approval during a control review?
A: Show answer
Select a completed period and trace it through relationship classification, source extraction, population completeness, data normalization, matching, opening-to-closing calculations, exception investigation, counterpart confirmation, independent review, authorized corrections, posting verification, final reporting, and archive. For selected transactions, trace forward from the source document into the reported balance and backward from the reported balance to the underlying records. Check whether totals and row counts remain explainable at each transition. Include ordinary matches, a grouped match, a disputed dimension, a timing item, a correction, and a foreign-currency item where relevant. Distinguish documentary evidence from verbal explanations. Record observed gaps, risk, control owner, corrective action, target date, and retest requirements. A walkthrough tests whether the process is actually performed as described; it does not establish that the written SOP has been formally adopted.

Study instruction:
- "Complete an end-to-end walkthrough and retain a traceability matrix linking each process stage to evidence and the control conclusion."

Key point:
- Walkthroughs test implementation, completeness, and evidence across the entire reconciliation cycle.
- Observed practice, documented procedure, and formal authorization are separate matters.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger process documentation
40. Q: What can the fictional education-award reconciliation demonstrate about unresolved dimensions and foreign-exchange differences?
A: Show answer
The supplied example describes an illustrative September reconciliation in which several transaction groups match, while a direct program cost has incompatible fund dimensions and a foreign-currency item has a reporting-currency discrepancy. The sample presents IDR 1,000,000 reciprocal amounts against different fund classifications and a EUR 1,000 transaction translated to IDR 17,000,000 on one side and IDR 17,200,000 on the other. The dimension dispute cannot be cleared merely because the amounts offset, and the conversion difference cannot be resolved without verifying the approved rate basis and source evidence. The fictional case therefore remains qualified rather than fully closed. Its amounts, IDs, mappings, and statuses are training data, not actual financial records. An actual reconciliation would require source evidence, an applicable accounting-policy decision, authorized corrections where needed, and a verified rerun.

Study instruction:
- "Reperform the fictional matching calculations and identify which issues must remain open despite offsetting values or matched transaction groups."

Key point:
- Unresolved fund classification and exchange-rate issues require separate investigation.
- Partial matching does not justify closing a relationship that still has material unresolved exceptions.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger records and reporting
41. Q: What can the fictional related-entity case demonstrate about reciprocal balances and consolidation adjustments?
A: Show answer
The example describes separate fictional legal entities with reciprocal balances of IDR 4,000,000 and negative IDR 4,000,000. Their arithmetic agrees, but the required independent counterpart confirmation is absent. The reconciliation therefore retains a confirmation-required status instead of treating the balance as fully closed. The example also assigns a separate consolidation interface record and states that the illustrative elimination is not posted to either entity's books. This demonstrates that three questions must be answered separately: whether the balances agree mathematically, whether the counterparty relationship and transaction are adequately supported, and whether any consolidation adjustment has been validly authorized. Actual accounting treatment depends on the applicable reporting framework and entity structure. None of the names, balances, records, or approvals in the source is evidence of a real affiliate or consolidation entry.

Study instruction:
- "Review the fictional affiliate case and document the separate requirements for reciprocal agreement, independent confirmation, and consolidation approval."

Key point:
- Reciprocal agreement does not remove a required confirmation control.
- Consolidation adjustments must remain distinct from entries in individual legal-entity books.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and financial reporting
42. Q: How can reconciliation templates and automation improve consistency without replacing professional judgment?
A: Show answer
Standardized worksheets can maintain relationship masters, source extract manifests, normalized transaction tables, match groups, signed balance bridges, exception registers, confirmation logs, review checklists, correction trackers, and management summaries. Power Query or equivalent data-transformation tools can combine source files, standardize fields, and flag duplicates or missing dimensions. Spreadsheet formulas can recalculate signed totals and balance bridges, while controlled automation can refresh reports and log processing results. Each transformation and matching rule should be tested against independently calculated examples, including negative fixtures and ambiguous cases. The tool must retain source references and explain exceptions rather than silently forcing records into a match. Accounting classification, donor eligibility, legal questions, and correction approval remain governed by the applicable authority and designated decision-makers. A successful refresh or macro execution is not evidence that the reconciliation is correct.

Study instruction:
- "Test a reconciliation workbook using independently verified results, including duplicates, incorrect dimensions, incomplete extracts, and failed matching rules."

Key point:
- Automation improves repeatability when input, calculation, exception, and approval controls are designed properly.
- Software must not make unauthorized accounting decisions or conceal unmatched records.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger and controlled journal workflows
43. Q: Which general ledger reports can support the reconciliation process, and how should they be used?
A: Show answer
The UN IPSAS Finance Manual's General Ledger chapter describes reports such as account line-item reports, detailed journal listings, and trial balance reports within the Umoja environment. In an appropriate system, these report types can help establish account populations, inspect transaction-level postings, and reconcile closing balances. The reports should be selected using the correct entity, ledger, period, account, fund, project, and other relevant dimensions. Preserve the report parameters and compare the extracted totals with the relevant control balances. A report is useful only when its selection criteria and status are appropriate for the reconciliation being performed. The Umoja transaction codes and system-specific steps in the manual should not automatically be assumed to apply to a different organization's ERP or accounting system; the actual system owner's documentation and access permissions must be followed.

Study instruction:
- "Select an account line-item report, a journal listing, and a trial balance for one period, then explain what each report can and cannot establish."

Key point:
- Different reports support transaction inspection, journal tracing, and balance-level verification.
- System-specific instructions must be validated against the actual accounting environment.

Source links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 18 — General Ledger reports
44. Q: How should the organization test, approve, and formally adopt a reconciliation SOP before operational use?
A: Show answer
Treat the document as a controlled procedure requiring validation of the actual entity structure, applicable accounting framework, source systems, chart of accounts, donor terms, responsibility assignments, approval limits, and escalation rules. Replace the fictional names, IDs, balances, evidence references, relationships, allocation drivers, dates, and system assumptions with verified organizational information. Walk through each workflow, test the associated decision rules, run independent calculations, and test negative cases for incomplete data, duplicate matches, incorrect dimensions, unconfirmed balances, and unauthorized corrections. Confirm that the document package, forms, evidence indexes, and version controls operate as intended. Independent review should document unresolved issues and acceptance criteria. Release requires a version number, owner, authorized approver, effective date, communication plan, training, and review schedule. A generated document, successful test calculation, or approval placeholder does not itself establish organizational adoption.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare an adoption checklist that verifies document integrity, actual-entity applicability, negative-test results, independent review, formal approval, and effective-date controls."

Key point:
- A fictional example is a design reference, not an operationally approved SOP.
- Formal adoption requires verified organizational facts, testing, authorized approval, and controlled release.

Table 9
Financial Statement Preparation and Disclosure Controls
1. Q: What is the purpose of an SOP for financial statement preparation and disclosure?
A: Show answer
A Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) establishes a controlled process for preparing, reconciling, reviewing, approving, issuing, correcting, and retaining financial statements and their notes. It connects source transactions and ledger balances to approved accounting policies, calculations, disclosures, judgments, and supporting evidence. The process must identify the reporting entity, purpose, period, applicable framework, accountable officers, independent reviewers, approval authority, and release requirements. A balanced trial balance or signed cover page alone does not demonstrate completeness or compliance. For an INGO, the procedure must reflect the organization's verified legal, contractual, donor, regulatory, and accounting requirements. The supplied DOCX is explicitly a fictional demonstration; its balances, roles, approvals, framework assumptions, and conclusions must not be treated as actual organizational facts.

Study instruction:
- "Map the complete reporting process from source data through final approval, issuance, and archive, identifying the evidence required at each stage."

Key point:
- Financial statement preparation requires traceable data, supported judgments, and independent review.
- A demonstration template is not an approved accounting policy or an actual financial statement.

Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Presentation of Financial Statements and Disclosures
2. Q: What types of financial reports must be distinguished before preparing a reporting package?
A: Show answer
Distinguish general-purpose financial statements, statutory filings, tax returns, donor financial reports, grant-budget reports, management accounts, governing-body reports, and audit deliverables. These reports may use overlapping data but serve different purposes and may follow different reporting bases, presentation rules, deadlines, and contractual requirements. Identify each report's intended users, governing authority, entity perimeter, period, accounting basis, currency, required content, and approval route. A donor report cannot automatically substitute for statutory financial statements, and a management dashboard cannot establish compliance with an external reporting obligation. Reconcile shared amounts while preserving the rules applicable to each reporting product. Maintain separate completion statuses so that finishing one report does not inadvertently mark another obligation complete.

Study instruction:
- "Classify the organization's reports by purpose, governing requirement, users, reporting basis, and approval authority."

Key point:
- Shared financial data does not make different reports interchangeable.
- Each reporting obligation requires its own completion criteria and evidence.

Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Presentation of Financial Statements and Disclosures
3. Q: How should an organization determine which accounting framework governs its financial statements?
A: Show answer
Confirm the legal entity, jurisdiction, legal form, applicable legislation, reporting purpose, contractual commitments, and any requirements imposed by regulators or governing authorities. Identify whether the applicable basis is Indonesian Financial Accounting Standards, IPSAS, IFRS as adopted or required locally, or another permitted framework. Obtain the current authorized standard, identify the relevant version and effective date, and document the applicability conclusion with an appropriate reviewer and authorized approver. Do not assume that an international NGO must apply IPSAS merely because its activities resemble those of a public-sector organization. Likewise, do not infer an accounting framework from a donor agreement, organizational label, or example template alone. If the framework remains unresolved, suspend affected accounting-policy decisions and prevent final approval or issuance until the issue is resolved.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a framework-applicability memorandum identifying the entity, legal requirements, candidate frameworks, authoritative evidence, decision, and approval."

Key point:
- Framework selection requires evidence about the actual reporting entity and its obligations.
- The UN IPSAS Finance Manual is an important reference but does not automatically govern every INGO.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual
- Indonesian Institute of Accountants — official standards portal
4. Q: How should accounting authorities, donor agreements, organizational policies, and working assumptions be prioritized?
A: Show answer
Establish an approved authority hierarchy that accounts for mandatory legislation and statutory reporting requirements, the confirmed accounting framework and its authoritative interpretations, applicable contractual and donor requirements, and authorized internal policies. A contract may impose additional reporting conditions, but it should not be assumed to override mandatory law or the selected accounting framework. Internal procedures and working assumptions must remain consistent with higher-level requirements. When authorities conflict, document the conflicting provisions, affected transactions, financial implications, legal or technical advice required, and the decision made by an authorized person. Do not resolve a material conflict through an undocumented spreadsheet formula or an informal instruction. Maintain a source register recording the official title, issuer, exact provision, jurisdiction, effective date, applicability rationale, reviewer, approval, and next review trigger.

Study instruction:
- "Trace one accounting decision to its governing standard, relevant agreement, internal policy, and documented approval."

Key point:
- Accounting conclusions must rest on applicable authority rather than assumptions.
- Conflicts require documented assessment and authorized resolution.

Official links:
- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Presentation of Financial Statements and Disclosures
5. Q: How should the reporting entity and consolidation perimeter be established?
A: Show answer
Define the legal entity whose statements are being prepared and identify the operations, branches, controlled entities, funds, programs, and other components included within the reporting perimeter. Examine legal ownership, decision-making authority, control relationships, relevant agreements, and the consolidation requirements of the applicable framework. Document why an entity is included or excluded and obtain approval for the conclusion. Distinguish a legal entity's own statements from group or consolidated statements, and avoid combining separate organizations merely because they share management, donors, premises, or bank arrangements. If consolidation is required, identify the reporting packages needed from each entity, accounting-policy consistency requirements, inter-entity balances, elimination procedures, and currency considerations. If the perimeter is unresolved, affected statements should remain on hold.

Study instruction:
- "Prepare a reporting-perimeter schedule listing all potentially relevant entities, the evidence supporting inclusion or exclusion, and the approval status."

Key point:
- Reporting scope must be based on applicable requirements and documented facts.
- Unsupported inclusion or exclusion can materially misstate financial position and performance.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Presentation of Financial Statements and Disclosures
6. Q: How should the reporting period, reporting date, currencies, comparatives, and required statements be confirmed?
A: Show answer
Before preparation begins, document the legal entity, reporting perimeter, reporting purpose, period start and end dates, reporting date, functional currency, presentation currency, comparative period, selected framework and edition, and required statement set. Confirm whether annual or interim reporting rules apply and determine whether additional statutory, donor, budgetary, or governing-body reports are required. Verify opening balances against the approved previous-period closing records and preserve the source of comparative figures. Do not use the reporting dates or currencies embedded in a fictional template without verification. If the framework, period, currency, or reporting perimeter changes, assess its effect on calculations, mappings, comparative presentation, disclosures, and approvals before continuing.

Study instruction:
- "Complete a reporting-package initiation form and reconcile every reporting parameter to authoritative organizational records."

Key point:
- Reporting parameters define the boundaries for the entire financial statement package.
- A change in period, perimeter, or framework can invalidate downstream work.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Presentation of Financial Statements and Disclosures
7. Q: What statements and supporting notes may form a complete financial statement package?
A: Show answer
Under the relevant reporting framework, a complete package may include a statement of financial position, statement of financial performance, statement of changes in net assets or equity, statement of cash flows, comparison of budget and actual amounts where required, and notes comprising significant accounting policies and other explanatory disclosures. The actual set depends on the applicable framework, reporting purpose, entity, and legal or contractual requirements. Additional statements or disclosures may be required for particular circumstances. Establish a statement inventory showing the required item, owner, source data, preparation status, review status, approval, and supporting evidence. Do not omit a required statement merely because the accounting system does not generate it automatically. Conversely, do not assume every illustrative statement is mandatory for every reporting entity.

Study instruction:
- "Compare the proposed statement inventory against the confirmed framework and reporting obligations, documenting the applicability conclusion for each component."

Key point:
- The required statement set must be established before preparation.
- A template's illustrative presentation is not a universal requirement.

- UN IPSAS Finance Manual, Chapter 16 — Presentation of Financial Statements and Disclosures
8. Q: How should the reporting calendar and responsibility matrix be established?
A: Show answer
Prepare a reporting calendar before the close that identifies source-data deadlines, reconciliation completion dates, adjustment preparation, technical review, management review, governance approval, external filing or issue deadlines, and archive completion. Assign an owner and accountable approver to each deliverable and identify dependencies between workstreams. Build realistic review time into the schedule rather than placing approval immediately after preparation. Distinguish statutory and contractual deadlines from internal target dates, and validate external deadlines through authoritative sources. Establish escalation triggers for missing data, unresolved material differences, unavailable approvers, and system interruptions. A missed internal deadline should lead to documented escalation and impact assessment; it does not waive mandatory controls or automatically change an external deadline.

Study instruction:
- "Create a period-close calendar with named owners, dependencies, review windows, verified external deadlines, and escalation arrangements."

Key point:
- Clear timing and ownership reduce last-minute reporting failures.
- Deadlines must be verified, not inferred from prior-period habits.
